

## 2026-11-11 — #112 *Asio abyssinicus* v4 — juvenile-down texture correction and Dinsho habitat boundary

### ABY-BMNP-BIRDING-2026 — newly indexed institutional habitat page
- **Title / institution / accessed:** “Birding — The Bale Birding Zones” / Bale Mountains National Park / 2026-11-11.
- **URL / section:** https://balemountains.org/plan-your-trip/activities/birding/ ; “BMNP Headquarters (Juniper woodlands)”, lines 55–72 in the accessed page.
- **Species/stage/place:** *Asio abyssinicus*; occurrence context at Dinsho, not an age or nest source.
- **Facts:** the headquarters patch is a remnant dry Afromontane forest dominated by *Hagenia abyssinica*, *Juniperus procera* and *Hypericum revolutum*; the park describes it as one of Ethiopia’s best places to see Abyssinian Long-eared Owl.
- **Limits/decision:** tourism/institution page, not a breeding or developmental study. Supports a Dinsho Afromontane forest setting and named vegetation only; does not support a nest, juvenile provenance, sex ratio or plumage detail.

### ABY-COLLAR-BOESMAN-2019 — reused, museum/taxonomy paper re-read
- Collar & Boesman, 2019, “The taxonomy of certain *Asio* and *Bubo* owl species in Africa and Arabia,” *Bulletin of the African Bird Club* 26(2):155–169.
- URL: https://www.researchgate.net/publication/335292194_The_taxonomy_of_certain_Asio_and_Bubo_owl_species_in_Africa_and_Arabia ; pp.158–161, Figs.1–3, Tables1–2.
- Broader/fused dark upperpart markings, dense irregular net-like underpart panels, curved pale spots on secondaries/tail and the more ochre-buff nominate phenotype remain the adult identity basis. Sexes are pooled; no fixed female-larger display is licensed.

### ABY-MATON-1989-JUV — reused, species-labelled field photograph re-opened from repository evidence
- Peter Maton, age-unknown juvenile *A. abyssinicus*, Ethiopia 1989: https://www.flickr.com/photos/34579661@N04/5424426357
- Visible boundary retained: charcoal/gray-brown **loose irregular** down, very dark face, orange irises, dark bill, short emerging tufts, concealed tail and natural support. The photograph does not establish exact age, sex, subspecies, flight competence, foot anatomy or wing/tail chronology.
- V3 reinspection found repeated tight decorative curls inconsistent with the evidence wording; v4 will replace them with wispy clumps and broken down edges, while keeping wings/tail compact and mostly concealed.

### ABY-OGADA-MURIITHI-2017 — reused, primary field-project report re-read
- Darcy Ogada & Paul Muriithi, 2017, “Is the Abyssinian Owl on Mt Kenya dependent on declining heath forest habitat?”, African Bird Club final report: https://www.africanbirdclub.org/sites/default/files/2016_Abyssinian_Owl.pdf
- Read pp.1–5. The report states there had been no systematic species study, breeding and movements were poorly known, and its Mt Kenya sightings/roost/pellets were preliminary and not conclusively assigned. It supports an extreme-highland woodland/heath context and uncertainty, not a nest or universal habitat claim.

### ABY-ONET-LANNELUC-2018 — reused, dated adult field photograph
- Catherine & Bernard Lanneluc, adult *A. abyssinicus*, Dinsho, November 2018: https://www.oiseaux.net/photos/catherine.et.bernard.lanneluc/hibou.d.abyssinie.1.html
- Supports orange irides, black bill, flexible central tufts, warm dark-rimmed facial disc, robust dark/ochre body pattern and feathered tarsi. One image does not determine sex or a universal tuft angle/colour.

### ABY-ITIS-555498-2026 — reused taxonomy record
- ITIS TSN 555498: https://itis.gov/servlet/SingleRpt/SingleRpt?print_version=PRT&search_topic=TSN&search_value=555498&source=to_print
- Current *Asio abyssinicus*; historical/original combination *Otus abyssinicus*; nominate *A. a. abyssinicus* kept distinct from *A. a. graueri*. Taxonomy only.

- **Index change:** new 1; reused/re-read 5; corrected inference boundaries 3 (sex scale, juvenile down/wing-tail, nest/habitat). User-uploaded Mikkola guide was not mounted in the current execution; no fresh page claim is added. Detailed facts, design inferences, complete prompt and unknowns: `owl-pet/research/species/112-asio-abyssinicus-v4.md`. Scientific status remains false.


## 2026-11-11 — #111 *Otus cyprius* v4 — adult-pattern correction and fledged-juvenile boundary

### CYSO-MIKKOLA-2013-P122-123
- **Title / author / year:** *Owls of the World: A Photographic Guide* / Heimo Mikkola / 2013.
- **Pages / accessed / type:** printed pp.122–123, uploaded PDF pp.124–125; freshly read 2026-11-11; photographic guide.
- **Species/stages:** historical *Otus scops cyprius* plus broader *O. scops* complex; adults, downy chick and age-unknown juvenile/mesoptile.
- **Facts:** Cyprus form called silvery-grey; complex account gives small tufts, black shaft-streaks, white scapular webs, barred flight/tail, yellow eyes, grey bill and tarsal feathering to toe bases; juvenile fluffier with prominent vermiculations.
- **Limits/decision:** broad complex size and female-heavier wording are not Cyprus-specific visible dimorphism; no exact juvenile age/flight claim. Supports old-name mapping, bare parts and a conservative fluff/vermiculation boundary.

### CYSO-FLINT-2015
- **Title / authors / year:** “Reprising the taxonomy of Cyprus Scops Owl *Otus (scops) cyprius*…” / Flint et al. / 2015.
- **URL / accessed / type:** https://www.britishbirds.co.uk/wp-content/uploads/article_files/V108/V108_N06/V108_N06_P291_307_A006.pdf ; 2026-11-11; primary taxonomic/morphology paper.
- **Pages:** diagnosis/morphology pp.298–301; measurements p.300; breeding/habitat pp.303–304.
- **Facts:** darkest/darker-grey taxon, heavy broad black streaking, coarse vermiculation, narrow bars and larger white spots; no rufous morph. Specimen wing means male n=10 153.3 mm, female n=13 153.5 mm; eggs Apr–May, chicks late Apr–Jul; villages/light woodland/open pine forest.
- **Limits/decision:** tiny live samples and no juvenile series; use near-equal adult height and irregular coarse pattern, not a fixed sex ratio.

### CYSO-SOUNDAPPROACH-2013PHOTOS
- **Title / institution:** “Cyprus Scops Owl” / The Sound Approach.
- **URL / accessed / type:** https://soundapproach.co.uk/cyprus-scops-owl/ ; 2026-11-11; reliable field-photo narrative.
- **Stage/facts:** ancient olive grove; juvenile fed by an adult on 21 June; several fledged juveniles photographed on 27 June; at least two young fledged.
- **Limits/decision:** no hatch age, sex, controlled flight test or continuous sequence. Supports an age-unknown, branch-supported fledged juvenile only.

### CYSO-BIRDLIFE-CYPRUS-2026
- **Title / institution:** “Cyprus Scops Owl” / BirdLife Cyprus.
- **URL / accessed / type:** https://birdlifecyprus.org/cyprus-scops-owl/ ; 2026-11-11; current institution overview.
- **Facts:** 19–21 cm, 47–54 cm wingspan; hollows in conifers/olive trees and boxes; forests, farmland, villages and olive groves.
- **Limits/decision:** overview, not an ageing source; habitat/nesting context only.

### CYSO-IEZEKIEL-ETAL-2021
- **Title / authors / year:** “Nest-Box Occupancy by the Cyprus Scops Owl in Different Habitats” / Iezekiel et al. / 2021, *Animals* 11:1775.
- **DOI / URL / accessed:** https://doi.org/10.3390/ani11061775 ; 2026-11-11.
- **Type / scope:** primary 2015–2018 breeding study; 238 boxes in Paphos Forest rural, ecotone and forest sites; laying through fledging.
- **Facts:** boxes mainly on *Pinus brutia* or buildings; 5–11% occupancy; edge/rural occupancy higher.
- **Limits/decision:** no juvenile feather chronology or locomotor threshold; breeding-site context only.

### Reuse and corrected boundaries
- New1 / reused4 / corrected5. V3's pale warm-grey and tidy narrow pinstripes are corrected toward dark cool grey, broad irregular streaks, coarse vermiculation and larger isolated white spots. Cyprus wing data do not justify imposed female height. A photographed fledged stage does not establish exact age or sustained flight. Olive-grove context is documented but not universal. Science remains false.
- Links: [v4 research](species/111-otus-cyprius-v4.md); planned v4; run `production/runs/20261111T175057+0800-cycle-5.md`.

## 2026-11-08 — #34 *Glaucidium hardyi* v5 — original-description table and primary cavity-young re-read

### Re-read / direct-page verification — APOW-VIELLIARD-1989
- **Title / author / year:** “Uma nova espécie de *Glaucidium* (Aves, Strigidae) da Amazônia” / Jacques M. E. Vielliard / 1989, *Revista Brasileira de Zoologia* 6(4):685–693.
- **DOI / direct PDF / accessed:** https://doi.org/10.1590/S0101-81751989000400014 ; https://www.biologicaldiversity.org/campaigns/global_owl_project/descriptions/Glaucidium/Glaucidium%20hardyi%20(Vielliard)%201990,%20Revue%20Brasil%20Zoologie,%206%20(1989),%20pages%20685-693..PDF ; accessed 2026-11-08.
- **Type / scope:** primary original species description; museum series and vocal diagnosis.
- **Pages actually viewed:** printed pp.691–692 (PDF pp.7–8), including Table I and holotype/species diagnosis.
- **Facts read/viewed:** Table I reports *G. hardyi* wing roughly 91–97 mm and tail 41–46 mm in the listed series; the one female wing value (94 mm) lies within male values, so the sample does not establish a general female-larger visual rule. The diagnosis emphasizes grey crown, large pale spots and plain rufous back, and separates the species from *G. minutissimum* by morphology and voice.
- **Limits / confidence:** tiny type series, not a population sex-morphometric study; no juvenile development. High for adult diagnosis and specimen ranges, low for population sex direction.
- **Design use:** near-equal adults; grey speckled head against plain warm rufous mantle/back; short tail and relatively long round wing.

### Re-read — APOW-DEVILLE-2012
- **Title / authors / year:** “Observations at a nest of Amazonian Pygmy Owl *Glaucidium hardyi* in French Guiana” / Tanguy Deville & Johan Ingels / 2012, *Neotropical Birding* 11:23–26.
- **URL / accessed:** https://tanguydeville.net/wp-content/uploads/2019/05/2012-02-observations-at-a-nest-of-amazonian-pygmy-owl-glaucidium-hardyi-in-french-guiana-neotropical-birding-nc2b011.pdf ; accessed 2026-11-08.
- **Type / scope:** primary field observation and photographs at one nest.
- **Pages / figures actually read:** pp.23–26, especially nest description pp.23–24 and Figs.2–4.
- **Facts:** old woodpecker hole about 25 m high in a dead c.35 m tree, entrance c.7.5 cm; it contained almost full-grown young. Only one young was ever seen at the entrance. Adults entered directly rather than pausing on the rim. Fig.2 documents adult occipital false eyes.
- **Limits / confidence:** one nest and one visually observed young; no exact age, iris chronology, flight threshold or continuous feather series. High for cavity context and “almost full-grown” stage only.
- **Design use:** one almost-full-grown juvenile fully supported at the vertical cavity lip; no flight, leap or independence claim.


## 2026-11-08 — #33 *Taenioptynx brodiei* v5 — primary-paper re-read, current combination and 56-day fledgling boundary

### Re-read / correction — COOW-LIN-ETAL-2014
- **Title / authors / year:** “Colour Morphs in the Collared Pygmy Owl *Glaucidium brodiei* are Age-Related, not a Polymorphism” / Wen-Loung Lin, Si-Min Lin & Hui-Yun Tseng / 2014, *Ardea* 102(1):95–99.
- **DOI / URL / accessed:** https://doi.org/10.5253/078.102.0115 ; author-uploaded full text read at https://www.researchgate.net/publication/264422734_Colour_Morphs_in_the_Collared_Pygmy_Owl_Glaucidium_brodiei_are_Age-Related_not_a_Polymorphism ; accessed 2026-11-08.
- **Type / scope:** primary longitudinal nest-box, banding and recapture study at Lien-hua-chih, central Taiwan, supplemented by 68 museum specimens.
- **Facts actually read:** 63 owls were banded, including 32 nestlings. A known individual hatched 15 June 2010 and at first recapture on 10 August (56 days) was fully grown but retained fledgling plumage: fewer bars/spots on back and crown and an incompletely formed occipital pattern. At 165 days it was rufous with formed occipital pattern; at 394 and 720 days it was grey. The study concludes fledgling plumage occurs at 2–3 months, rufous at 4–7 months and definitive plumage at about 10–11 months. Adult/breeding-pair mass direction is male lighter than female, but measured ranges overlap (male 52–72 g; female 58–92 g).
- **Pages / figures:** printed pp.95–99; methods and mass p.96; results/discussion and Fig.3 pp.97–98.
- **Correction applied:** do not treat rufous and grey as stable sex-independent adult colour morphs. V5 uses definitive grey adults and a known-age 56-day fledgling with reduced dorsal/crown pattern and incomplete occipital marking. The overlapping masses permit only a subtle fuller female impression, not a universal height ratio.
- **Limits:** Taiwan study population; no quantitative wing/tail growth series or first-flight measurement. “Fully grown” does not establish independent flight performance. High confidence for the documented plumage sequence, moderate transfer outside the studied population.
- **Links:** research/species/33-taenioptynx-brodiei-v5.md; design/concepts/33-taenioptynx-brodiei-v5.png.

### Re-read / correction — COOW-ITIS-2026
- **Institution / records / accessed:** Integrated Taxonomic Information System; *Taenioptynx brodiei brodiei* TSN 1244720 and *Glaucidium brodiei brodiei* TSN 687017; accessed 2026-11-08.
- **URLs:** https://itis.gov/servlet/SingleRpt/SingleRpt?print_version=PRT&search_topic=TSN&search_value=1244720&source=to_print ; https://www.itis.gov/servlet/SingleRpt/SingleRpt?print_version=PRT&search_topic=TSN&search_value=687017&source=to_print
- **Facts actually read:** *Taenioptynx brodiei* is the valid species combination in the current hierarchy; *Glaucidium brodiei brodiei* is an invalid subsequent combination mapped to *Taenioptynx brodiei brodiei*. IOC World Bird List v15.1 is among the current record sources.
- **Limits / design use:** nomenclature only; does not support plumage, development or sex-size claims. Use current label *Taenioptynx brodiei* and preserve *Glaucidium brodiei* (and project historical *Noctua brodiei*) in the crosswalk.


## 2026-11-08 — #31 *Glaucidium radiatum* v5 — current name, cavity context and older-immature boundary

### JUOW-ITIS-2025
- **Title / institution / accessed:** “ITIS Report: *Glaucidium radiatum*” / Integrated Taxonomic Information System / accessed 2026-11-08.
- **URL:** https://itis.gov/servlet/SingleRpt/SingleRpt?print_version=PRT&search_topic=TSN&search_value=555463&source=to_print
- **Type / scope:** government-integrated taxonomic authority; species name and synonymy.
- **Facts read:** *Glaucidium radiatum* (Tickell, 1833) is valid; *Strix radiata* Tickell, 1833 is a synonym; two direct children are *G. r. radiatum* and *G. r. malabaricum*.
- **Section / lines:** Taxonomy and Nomenclature, retrieved lines 6–16 and 45–49.
- **Limits / confidence:** supports nomenclature, not plumage, development or sex-size direction. High confidence for name mapping.
- **Design use:** current label *Glaucidium radiatum*; retain *Strix radiata* as historical mapping; do not use taxonomy as morphology evidence.

### JUOW-MADRAS-MUSEUM-1979
- **Title / author / year / accessed:** *Birds’ Eggs and Nests in the Collection of the Madras Government Museum* / S. Thomas Satyamurti, Government Museum Madras / 1979 / accessed 2026-11-08.
- **URL:** https://govtmuseumchennai.org/uploads/topics/16528786805140.pdf
- **Type / scope:** government museum catalogue with nidification notes; nominate *G. r. radiatum*.
- **Facts read:** wooded/forest tracts; April–May breeding with earlier records; two to four white eggs in hollow trees; natural holes are usual, deserted barbet/woodpecker holes less frequent; thin deciduous and dense evergreen forest both recorded.
- **Page / lines:** printed p.145, retrieved lines 2556–2564.
- **Limits / confidence:** historical synthesis; no juvenile plumage, growth series or sexed measurements. High confidence for cavity and habitat context, moderate for broad breeding summary.
- **Design use:** natural deciduous-tree cavity background only; no exposed nest or invented nesting material.

### JUOW-OISEAUXNET-LEBOFF-2026
- **Title / author / date / accessed:** “Jungle Owlet — gale387974” / Anne and Gabriel Leboff / photograph dated January 2026; page accessed 2026-11-08.

### JUOW-OISEAUXBIRDS-BOUGLOUAN-2026
- **Title / author / accessed:** “Jungle Owlet” / Nicole Bouglouan, Oiseaux-Birds / accessed 2026-11-08.
- **URL:** https://www.oiseaux-birds.com/card-jungle-owlet.html
- **Type / scope:** secondary species synthesis citing HBW, König/Weick and Mikkola.
- **Facts read:** adults 20–22 cm; round untufted head, yellow eyes, green-yellow/yellow-gray bill, dense barring, rufous upperwing, no false eyes; male and female similar; juvenile has less distinct underpart barring and browner tail bars; mixed deciduous/secondary habitat and tree cavities.
- **Section / lines:** Description and Habitat, retrieved lines 84–121; juvenile and sex statements lines 112–119.
- **Limits / confidence:** secondary synthesis, not a sexed measurement table or continuous development series. Moderate-high for identity, moderate for juvenile summary.
- **Design use:** near-equal same-plumage adults; older immature with softer lower barring and browner short tail; no categorical female-larger claim.
## 2026-11-08 — #30 *Glaucidium cuculoides* v5 — official Hong Kong fledgling boundary and nest-context restraint

### ASBO-HKBWS-AVIFAUNA-2024
- **Title / institution / accessed:** “Asian Barred Owlet *Glaucidium cuculoides*” / The Avifauna of Hong Kong, Hong Kong Bird Watching Society / updated 2024-01-09; accessed 2026-11-08.
- **URL:** https://avifauna.hkbws.org.hk/species/0190/026900
- **Type / scope:** institutional species account; Hong Kong population, identification, habitat, breeding and undated juvenile development.
- **Facts read:** adults are 22–25 cm, rounded and untufted with yellow eyes, strong fine barring, white lower underparts with irregular brown marks and no face-like nape pattern; juveniles are spotted on crown/forehead. Most nests are natural tree holes 3–8 m high, especially Chinese Banyan. Fledglings occur near nest sites 3 May–3 June. Undated nest-box photographs show a final juvenile-plumage stage with yellow irides.
- **Section / lines:** Identification and Breeding; lines 6–16 and 45–54 in the retrieved account.
- **Limits / confidence:** authoritative regional synthesis, not a continuous measured growth series. Exact age, flight threshold and sex remain unknown. High confidence for Hong Kong *G. c. whiteleyi*; transfer to all subspecies is limited.
- **Design use:** adult identity; no false eyes; an undated yellow-eyed juvenile with spotted crown/forehead; natural banyan cavity context and no exact-day claim.
- **Links:** research/species/30-glaucidium-cuculoides-v5.md; design/concepts/30-glaucidium-cuculoides-v5.png.

### ASBO-HKBWS-HKBR-2009-10
- **Title / authors / year / accessed:** *Hong Kong Bird Report 2009–10: Systematic List* / HKBWS, editors John A. Allcock, Geoff J. Carey, Gary Chow and Geoff Welch / published 2012; accessed 2026-11-08.
- **URL:** https://www.hkbws.org.hk/web/chi/documents/bird_report/2009-10_birdreport_protected.pdf
- **Type / scope:** institutional annual bird report with dated field record and plate.
- **Facts read:** at least one Asian Barred Owlet was present in Lam Tsuen valley during 2010 and a fledged juvenile was photographed on 5 June; Plate 22 shows an Asian Barred Owlet on a naturally supporting diagonal branch. This supports a branch-perched fledged stage, but not exact age or a flight-performance claim.
- **Page / lines:** printed p.127–128; retrieved lines 4514–4532; Plate 22.
- **Limits / confidence:** the text records a fledged juvenile, while the adjacent plate caption is a separate Kam Tin adult dated 10 May 2009. Do not treat Plate 22 as the 5 June juvenile. High confidence for occurrence/stage, limited visual detail for the juvenile.
- **Design use:** recently fledged juvenile may be shown quietly gripping a diagonal branch near the nest, with no airborne or independence claim.
- **Links:** research/species/30-glaucidium-cuculoides-v5.md; design/concepts/30-glaucidium-cuculoides-v5.png.

### ASBO-SANKAMETHAWEE-PIERCE-2019
- **Title / authors / year / accessed:** “Ground-nesting Attempt by Asian Barred Owlet *Glaucidium cuculoides*” / Wangworn Sankamethawee & Andrew J. Pierce / 2019; accessed 2026-11-08.
- **URL:** https://www.researchgate.net/profile/Wangworn-Sankamethawee/publication/342520694_A_Ground_nesting_attempt_by_Asian_Barred_Owlet_Glaucidium_cuculoides/links/5ef9690592851c52d60698a0/A-Ground-nesting-attempt-by-Asian-Barred-Owlet-Glaucidium-cuculoides.pdf
- **Type / scope:** peer-reviewed primary field note; exceptional failed egg-stage ground nesting in Thailand.
- **Facts read:** the species normally uses natural tree cavities or old woodpecker/barbet holes; the documented exposed ground attempt failed. Body mass is summarized as about 150–240 g.
- **Pages / lines:** pp.157–160; retrieved lines 0–28.
- **Limits / confidence:** supplies no juvenile plumage or sex-size calibration; exceptional failed ground nesting must not be generalized.
- **Design use:** preserve cavity-tree context and explicitly avoid depicting a successful ground nest.
- **Links:** research/species/30-glaucidium-cuculoides-v5.md.

## 2026-11-06 — #112 *Asio abyssinicus* v3 — nominate-adult identity and older-juvenile stage

### ABY-ITIS-555498-2026 — newly indexed current/old-name record
- Integrated Taxonomic Information System, TSN 555498, accessed 2026-11-06: https://itis.gov/servlet/SingleRpt/SingleRpt?print_version=PRT&search_topic=TSN&search_value=555498&source=to_print
- Current valid *Asio abyssinicus*; original/invalid combination *Otus abyssinicus*. Two subspecies are listed: nominate *A. a. abyssinicus* and *A. a. graueri*. Taxonomy only; no morphology/development inference. High confidence for naming.

### ABY-COLLAR-BOESMAN-2019 — newly indexed primary museum/taxonomy study
- N. J. Collar & Peter Boesman, 2019, “The taxonomy of certain *Asio* and *Bubo* owl species in Africa and Arabia,” *Bulletin of the African Bird Club* 26(2):155–169: https://www.researchgate.net/publication/335292194_The_taxonomy_of_certain_Asio_and_Bubo_owl_species_in_Africa_and_Arabia
- Pages 158–161, Figs. 1–3, Tables 1–2 actually read. Compared with *A. otus*, *A. abyssinicus* has broader/fused dark upperpart markings, a denser net of underpart panels, curve-shaped pale spots rather than fine barring on secondaries/tail, and substantially larger bill/wing/tail means. Nominate birds tend more ochre-buff than smaller, greyer *graueri*. Sexes were pooled and only approximately balanced; this source does not support a fixed female-larger display. High confidence for specimen morphology; not a development source.

### ABY-MATON-1989-JUV — newly indexed species-labelled field photograph
- Peter Maton, “African Long-eared Owl (juv) (*Asio abyssinicus*),” photographed Ethiopia 1989; scanned slide uploaded 2011: https://www.flickr.com/photos/34579661@N04/5424426357
- Page and full browser image actually opened. Visible age-unknown older juvenile has charcoal/gray-brown loose down, a very dark face, orange irises, a dark bill with a narrow pale midface highlight, short emerging tufts, down-softened sides, concealed tail and branch/foliage support. The crop does not establish hatch age, sex, wing/tail chronology, foot anatomy or sustained flight. Medium confidence for visible stage and source label; not an ageing study.

### ABY-OGADA-MURIITHI-2017 — corrected/re-read field-project habitat limits
- Darcy Ogada & Paul Muriithi, 2017, African Bird Club final report, “Is the Abyssinian Owl on Mt Kenya dependent on declining heath forest habitat?”: https://www.africanbirdclub.org/sites/default/files/2016_Abyssinian_Owl.pdf
- Pages 1–5 actually read. The report documents extreme-highland distribution, a 1961 Kenya museum specimen from Hagenia woodland, preliminary suspected observations/roost evidence in heath and the authors’ explicit uncertainty about breeding/natural history. Design use is a generic montane-highland woodland/heath context only; suspected Mt Kenya roost/pellets cannot be turned into a confirmed nest or universal habitat. Medium-high for the project’s stated limits, low for species assignment of unconfirmed signs.

### ABY-ONET-LANNELUC-2018 — newly indexed dated adult photograph
- Catherine & Bernard Lanneluc, adult *A. abyssinicus*, Nov. 2018, Dinsho, Bale Mountains National Park, Ethiopia: https://www.oiseaux.net/photos/catherine.et.bernard.lanneluc/hibou.d.abyssinie.1.html
- Full field photograph and metadata opened. Supports nominate adult’s orange iris, black bill, tall but flexible central tufts, dark-rimmed warm facial disc, robust dark-gold streaked/barred body and feathered tarsi. One pose and lighting do not define universal colour, sex or tuft angle. Medium confidence for visible identity.

- Reused repository records: `ABO-COLLAR-BOESMAN-2019`, `ABO-OGADA-MURIITHI-2017` where present in v2 history; this update adds stable IDs without duplicating unsupported facts. User-uploaded Mikkola guide was not mounted in this execution, so no new page claim. Detailed boundaries and complete prompt: `research/species/112-asio-abyssinicus-v3.md`. Science remains false.

## 2026-11-06 — #105 *Ptilopsis granti* v8 — morphology/taxonomy re-read

### SWFO-ADW-2014 — newly indexed, institutional secondary account opened
- Title/author/institution: *Ptilopsis granti*; Amanda O'Farrill; Animal Diversity Web / University of Michigan
- Updated/year/type: 2014 account; institutional secondary synthesis
- URL: https://animaldiversity.org/accounts/Ptilopsis_granti/
- Accessed: 2026-11-06
- Read sections: Physical Description lines 57–64; Reproduction lines 87–119
- Facts used: white/pale-grey black-rimmed face, orange-red eyes, long streaked tufts, pale cream bill, grey streaked plumage; leg feathers extend to basal half of toes with dusky distal toes/dark claws; male 185–240 g and female 215–275 g ranges overlap; downy-white chicks/yellow-grey eyes and less strongly patterned fledged young.
- Limits/conflicts: secondary chronology and an obvious length-unit typo at lines 77–79; do not use its exact age schedule or fixed height ratio. Overlapping mass ranges forbid mechanical female-larger composition.
- Design use: near-equal adult height with subtly fuller female; basal-toe feathering; older juvenile less patterned with yellow-grey eyes.
- Confidence: medium for synthesis; lower for chronology.

### SWFO-ITIS-686682 — newly indexed, taxonomy record opened

- Limits: taxonomy only; no morphology/development inference.

- Confidence: high for taxonomy.
- Reused: SWFO-JWVH-2024 hospital p.16/PDF17 older-juvenile photograph record; exact age/flight remains unknown. Featherbase timed out and was not used.
- Mikkola upload unavailable; no new claim. Science false.

## 2026-11-06 — #104 *Asio madagascariensis* v5 — full primary chapter plus dated stage photographs

### MLO-RDR-GOODMAN2003 — newly indexed, original chapter read
- Title: “*Asio madagascariensis*, Madagascar Long-eared Owl”
- Authors/institution: L.-A. Rene de Roland & S. M. Goodman; in *The Natural History of Madagascar*
- Year/source type: 2003; primary breeding-study chapter
- URL: https://protectedareas.mg/content/documents/0cd9156e-e528-4293-b8da-d00aa5f5af4f/dc17b8fd22bd4c08ad8fcddd92d3ff17.pdf
- Accessed: 2026-11-06
- Species/stage: *Asio madagascariensis*; adults, eggs, nestlings, branching young
- Pages/figure: printed pp.1116–1117; Fig.12.20
- Read facts: one female weighed 770 g; no male weights, only “smaller than females”; one complete 2000 breeding cycle used an epiphyte tuft about 9 m high; young self-fed at 25 days, moved around/off the nest into adjacent trees at 36 days, remained near and returned for prey, first fed away from the nest at 42 days, moved 50–70 m thereafter and dispersed at 64 days. Fig.12.20 is a separate Tsinjoarivo fledgling molting from white nestling to dark adult plumage.
- Evidence boundary/conflict: the one 770 g female plus unquantified male statement does not support a fixed display ratio. The figure is not the measured Ambanizana brood; do not assign its plumage photograph an exact age or turn branch movement into demonstrated sustained flight.
- Design/check use: near-equal adult height with only modest female fullness; epiphyte fork as a recorded case; age-unknown older branch-supported mesoptile with compact formed wings, no flight pose.
- Confidence: high for cited study facts; limited to one followed pair/brood and one separate figure.

- Species/stage: juvenile; exact age/sex unknown
- Page/figure: file page and original 4478×3358 image
- Read facts: page identifies juvenile, Andasibe locality, date and author. The opened photograph supports white/cream fluff, dark facial mask, short tufts, formed brown folded wings and branch grip.
- Limitations: no hatch age, sex, iris chronology, molt series or flight test; image evidence is stage direction, not a timetable.
- Design/check use: retain formed but juvenile-soft wings and continuous branch support; label age unknown.
- Confidence: medium-high for visible form and metadata.

## 2026-11-06 — #103 *Tyto longimembris* v5 — expanded primary development reading

### EGO-DUNLOP2016 — corrected/expanded existing record
- Title: Reproduction, social behaviour and captive husbandry in the eastern grass owl (*Tyto longimembris*)
- Authors: Ric Dunlop; Philip Pain
- Year/source: 2016, *Journal of Zoo and Aquarium Research* 4(4):169–173
- DOI/URL: https://doi.org/10.19227/jzar.v4i4.166 ; https://jzar.org/jzar/article/download/166/117/1250
- Accessed: 2026-11-06
- Type: original captive study; open full text actually read
- Species/stage: Australian *T. longimembris*; nestlings, approximately 55–69 day mesoptile/first plumage, captive adults
- Pages/figures/tables: pp.169–173; p.171 Table 1; p.172 Fig.1 and plumage section; p.172–173 Table 3
- Facts: natal down dries cream-white; rich golden-brown powder-puff mesoptile appears from about day 55 through day 65; later first plumage is dark tawny above with white tips and mid-tawny below, and the face at down-loss is dark tawny with white tips. Fig.1 identifies a female chick at 55 and 69 days. Table 3 reports unsexed-chick measurements at days 1/46/57/69 but has no wing/tail value at day 57; wing is 116 mm at day 46 and 215 mm at day 69, tail 35 and 67 mm. The paper explicitly cautions about captive observations.
- Decision: retain the day-55 captive-reference label, rich golden second down and partial emerging facial feathers; do not interpolate day-57 wing/tail or claim fledging/flight.
- Limits/confidence: high for the described captive birds/stages; limited generalization to wild timing or population-wide development.
- Links: research/103-tyto-longimembris-v5.md; design/concepts/103-tyto-longimembris-v5.png

## 2026-11-08 — #102 *Strix nigrolineata* v5 — dimorphism, taxonomy and scope correction

### BWO-GERHARDT-GERHARDT-1997-USFS
- **Title/authors/institution:** “Size, Dimorphism, and Related Characteristics of Ciccaba Owls From Guatemala”; Richard P. Gerhardt & Dawn McAnnis Gerhardt; USDA Forest Service / 2nd Owl Symposium.
- **Year/URL:** 1997; https://www.nrs.fs.usda.gov/pubs/gtr/gtr_nc190/gtr_nc190_190.pdf ; institutional record https://research.fs.usda.gov/treesearch/15479
- **Accessed/type:** 2026-11-08; primary field-study conference paper; full PDF read.
- **Scope/stage/location:** breeding adults and reproductive/ecological data, Tikal National Park, Guatemala; printed pp.190–196, especially pp.191–192.
- **Useful facts:** one measured Black-and-white Owl pair: female 535 g, wing 29.3 cm, tail 18.7 cm; male 436 g, 28.6 cm, 16.5 cm. Mass/tail differed more than wing; other pairs looked similarly different but were not captured. All pellets had insect remains and 74% also vertebrate remains. Four eggs averaged 33.8 ± 2.3 g; four clutches each contained one egg.
- **Limits/conflict/confidence:** high confidence for the measured pair and quoted sample; only one measured pair, so no population ratio. Body mass is not a standing-height multiplier. Diet/egg facts do not support adding prey or eggs to the identity plate.
- **Design decision/links:** fuller-bodied, subtly longer-tailed female at near-equal crown height; no prey/egg. Links: `research/species/102-strix-nigrolineata-v5.md`, planned `design/concepts/102-strix-nigrolineata-v5.png`.

- **Accessed/type:** 2026-11-08; primary field paper; original PDF read.
- **Scope/stage/location:** one nesting Black-and-white Owl pair, northern Guatemala; *Journal of Field Ornithology* 65(2):258–264.
- **Useful facts:** insects were present in all examined pellets and bat remains were frequent; ecological context only.
- **Limits/confidence:** one pair; no juvenile morphology, feather sequence or sex-size sample. High for reported diet, not relevant to depicting a prey item.

## 2026-11-06 — #100 *Strix huhula* v5 — directly described nestling/brancher boundary

### HUH-BC2013
- **Title / authors / year:** “Distribution, nesting, and vocalizations of the Black-banded Owl (*Ciccaba huhula albomarginata*) in Argentina” — Alejandro Bodrati & Kristina Cockle, 2013.
- **URL / pages / accessed:** https://sora.unm.edu/sites/default/files/ON%2024%282%29%20169-182%20NEW.pdf ; *Ornitología Neotropical* 24:169–182; printed pp.169–182, especially p.175; full PDF re-read 2026-11-06.
- **Type / species / stage / facts:** primary nest monitoring of the Atlantic-forest subspecies under the historical combination *Ciccaba huhula albomarginata*. At the live angico tree fork, 15.2 m high and enclosed by vertical branches plus epiphytes, the 18 November nestling was mostly pale grey on body and wings with horizontal black barring, a black heart-shaped mask with white border, and yellow bill/legs; it walked actively. Pale grey still dominated on 27 November.
- **Limits / design use:** observations from one nest and one young; exact hatch date and continuous feather-tract measurements are absent. Supports a pale-grey-dominant, actively standing branch/nest-floor young with short soft-looking wing/tail geometry and continuous plant/branch support, not an exact-age or flight claim.

### HUH-MUNOZ-2019
- **Title / authors / year:** “Breeding records and notes on parental care of Black-and-White Owl (*Ciccaba huhula*) in Colombia” — Jorge Muñoz-García, Cristian Camilo Castaño Vargas, Sergio Reyes-Bueno & David Ricardo Rodríguez-Villamil, 2019.
- **URL / pages / accessed:** https://ufdcimages.uflib.ufl.edu/UF/00/10/09/58/00058/Spizaetus-28-%20English.pdf ; *Spizaetus* Issue 28, pp.14–19, especially printed pp.16–17/Figs.3–5; full article re-read 2026-11-06.
- **Type / stage / facts:** primary field record. A young found on the ground on 30 August was placed on a low branch; both parents attended and provisioned it. By 7 September it was in the canopy making short flights between branches.
- **Limits / design use:** exact age, sex and feather dimensions were not measured; the one-week observation interval does not establish first-flight age. Supports a branch-capable age-unknown older fledgling only, not a precise chronology or universal periurban nest context.

### HUH-ITIS-1063256-2026
- **Institution / title / year:** Integrated Taxonomic Information System, “*Strix huhula* (Daudin, 1800), TSN 1063256,” current record read 2026-11-06.
- **URL / section:** https://itis.gov/servlet/SingleRpt/SingleRpt?print_version=PRT&search_topic=TSN&search_value=1063256 ; Taxonomy and Nomenclature.
- **Type / facts:** institutional taxonomy index. Current valid name *Strix huhula*; synonym *Ciccaba huhula*; recognized subspecies include *S. h. albomarginata* and *S. h. huhula*.
- **Limits / design use:** taxonomy mapping only; no morphology, sex, age, behavior or nest evidence.

### Reuse and corrected boundaries
- Reused repository extraction `HUH-MIK2013-340` for adult identity (round head/no tufts, dark iris, yellow bill, fine pale waves); the user-uploaded guide itself was unavailable in this execution, so no fresh page read is claimed.
- Corrected five boundaries: one monitored nest ≠ universal nest form; stage dates ≠ exact hatch age; active walking ≠ flight competence; one-week later short flights ≠ first-flight date; adult labels ≠ visible sex diagnosis or fixed female-larger ratio.
- New3 / reused1 / corrected5; selected design stage is the directly described pale-grey-dominant 18–27 November nest young, shown standing with both feet continuously supported and no flight. Scientific sign-off remains false.
- Links: `research/species/100-strix-huhula-v5.md`; planned v5; run `production/runs/20261106T105700+0800-cycle-1.md`.

## 2026-11-06 — #96 *Aegolius ridgwayi* v5 — recent-fledgling / natural-cavity boundary

### RID-CARMAN-SILES-ANGULO-2025-P127-128
- **Title / authors / year:** “Nesting ecology of the Unspotted Saw-whet Owl (*Aegolius ridgwayi*) in Costa Rica” — Carman, Siles & Angulo-Irola, 2025 conference abstract.
- **URL / pages / accessed:** https://www.raptorresearchfoundation.org/wp-content/uploads/2025/10/FINAL-2025-RRF-CONFERENCE-PROGRAM.pdf ; printed pp.127–128; read 2026-11-06.
- **Type / species / stage:** primary 2014–2024 nest monitoring summary; adults, eggs and nestling period.
- **Facts:** 11 active nests at 2,800–3,200 m; nine natural damage/decay cavities (seven *Escallonia myrtilloides*, two *Quercus costaricensis*) and two boxes; natural sites 4–15 m high; clutch two; incubation 28–30 d; nestling period 30–32 d.
- **Limits / design use:** conference abstract without individual plumage chronology; the population nestling period cannot age a photographed juvenile. Supports a natural-decay cavity/highland context and an age-unknown post-cavity stage only.

### RID-EISERMANN-AVENDANO-2018-P178
- **Title / authors / year:** *The owls of Guatemala* — Knut Eisermann & Claudia Avendaño, 2018.
- **URL / page / accessed:** https://www.biodiversitylibrary.org/page/56222494 ; BBOC 138, printed p.178; read 2026-11-06.
- **Type / facts:** peer-reviewed regional synthesis with breeding records; mainly Guatemala highlands 1,900–3,700 m and first breeding records in 2008–2015.
- **Limits / design use:** distribution/breeding context, not a juvenile ageing source.

### RID-ITIS-686697-2026
- **Institution / URL / accessed:** ITIS, TSN 686697; https://itis.gov/servlet/SingleRpt/SingleRpt?search_topic=TSN&search_value=686697 ; accessed 2026-11-06.
- **Facts:** valid *Aegolius ridgwayi*; synonym/name records include *Cryptoglaux ridgwayi* and historical infraspecific combinations.
- **Limits / design use:** taxonomy mapping only.

### RID-AVIBASE-RIDGWAYI-2026
- **Institution / URL / accessed:** Birds Canada/Avibase; https://avibase.bsc-eoc.org/species.jsp?avibaseid=2FE709CA54EADE7E ; accessed 2026-11-06.
- **Facts:** protonym *Cryptoglaux ridgwayi* Alfaro, 1905; type locality near Escazú, Costa Rica.
- **Limits / design use:** nomenclature only.

### RID-CAYAYA-EISERMANN-JUV-PHOTO-2026
- **Title / author / URL / accessed:** juvenile *Aegolius ridgwayi* photograph — Knut Eisermann / Bird Studies Guatemala (Cayaya Birding); https://www.cayaya-birding.com/juvenile-unspotted-saw-whet-owl-aegolius-ridgwayi/ ; image viewed 2026-11-06.
- **Type / stage / facts:** reliable field photograph; age-unknown down-rich older juvenile/recent fledgling with warm-brown body, pale facial markings, bright yellow eyes, compact short/hidden wing-tail geometry and natural branch support.
- **Limits / design use:** no exact date, age, sex or flight test; visual boundary only, not proof of 30–32 d age or flight ability.

### Reuse and corrected boundaries
- Reused repository extract `RID-MIK2013-446`; uploaded guide itself was unavailable, so no fresh page read is claimed.
- Corrected four boundaries: nestling-period mean ≠ individual age; branch presence ≠ flight competence; one Costa Rican cavity sample ≠ universal nest template; adult labels ≠ visible sex-dimorphism proof. New5 / reused1 / corrected4; scientific status false.
- Links: `research/species/96-aegolius-ridgwayi-v5.md`; planned v5; run `production/runs/20261106T093650+0800-cycle-4.md`.
## 2026-11-06 — #83 *Otus brucei* v5 — Jordan recent-fledgling boundary and current/old-name mapping

### BRU-KHOURY-ALSHAMLIH-DHEIB-2021-P286-287
- **Title / authors / year:** “The first breeding record of Pallid Scops Owl *Otus brucei* in Jordan” — Fares Khoury, Mohammed Alshamlih & Ahmad Dheib, 2021.
- **URL / pages / accessed:** https://www.researchgate.net/publication/357327177_The_first_breeding_record_of_Pallid_Scops_Owl_Otus_brucei_in_Jordan ; author-uploaded full text, *Sandgrouse* 43:286–287, especially printed p.287; read 2026-11-06.
- **Type / species / stage:** primary field record; two recently fledged *O. brucei* juveniles in mature Jordan Valley palm plantation.
- **Facts:** little ear tufts, rather short tail, fine breast/belly cross-bars without streaks, hissing/begging calls; birds observed together above the observers.
- **Limits/conflicts:** short night observation and low-quality plate; no hatch date, sex, measured feather geometry or sustained-flight test.
- **Design/check decision:** supported RECENT FLEDGLING, little tufts, short tail, fine transverse underpart bars and open-beak begging; no exact age or flight threshold.
- **Links:** `owl-pet/research/species/83-otus-brucei-v5.md`; planned v5; `owl-pet/production/runs/20261106T043000+0800-cycle-1.md`.

- **Facts:** valid *Otus brucei* (Hume, 1872); synonym/original combination *Ephialtes brucei* Hume, 1872; four listed subspecies.

## 2026-11-05 — #65 *Tyto soumagnei* v5 — no-visible-sex-dimorphism / down-only nestling boundary

### MRO-RAMAMONJISOA-2014-P56-57-75-77
- **Title / author / year:** *Étude de la biologie et de l’écologie d’Effraie de Soumagne Tyto soumagnei (Grandidier, 1878) dans la Nouvelle Aire Protégée de Bemanevika, Bealanana* — Juliot Carl Ramamonjisoa, Université d’Antananarivo doctoral thesis, defended 2014.
- **URL / pages / accessed:** https://protectedareas.mg/content/documents/ff1f419c-05a9-4910-88d5-22fde787ce37/f49d347156d2438f97d2c6b8df277bc1.pdf ; printed pp.56–57 and 75–77, plus Annex I; full PDF text and relevant passages read 2026-11-05.
- **Type / species / stages:** primary multi-year field thesis; eight sexed adults and three tree-hole nests, including down-covered nestlings of unknown exact age.
- **Facts:** the thesis explicitly states that no size, colour or form difference is visually detectable between male and female. In the measured sample, mean wingspan was nearly equal (male 771.5 ± 10.9 mm, female 769.5 ± 20.4 mm; n=4 each), while mean mass differed (male 298 ± 5.7 g, female 337.5 ± 10.4 g). Three nests were tree holes; entrances were 16–20 m high and internal depth 45–117 cm. In 2014, two chicks disappeared while still covered only in down and could not escape the cavity.
- **Limits / conflicts:** small adult sample; sexing used mass plus cloacal protuberance, not genetic sexing. The down-only chicks lack exact age, measurements, photographs in the extracted passage and feather chronology. Mass difference does not license a visible crown-height or silhouette ratio; inability to escape does not establish an exact locomotor threshold.
- **Design/check decision:** adults share equal display height and essentially the same silhouette/plumage; label sex without coding colour as sex. Depict one age-unknown down-covered nestling low on a deep cavity floor, with no long remiges, tail or branch/flight pose. The Bemanevika tree hole is a documented example, not universal habitat.
- **Links:** `research/species/65-tyto-soumagnei-v5.md`; planned concept v5; run `production/runs/20261105T153530+0800-cycle-1.md`.

### MRO-MNHN-HOLOTYPE-2026
- **Title / institution:** holotype specimen MNHN-ZO-MO-1888-640 — Muséum national d’Histoire naturelle, Paris.
- **URL / accessed / type:** https://science.mnhn.fr/institution/mnhn/collection/zo/item/mo-1888-640 ; institutional collection record and specimen images page read 2026-11-05; no printed page.
- **Taxonomy facts:** original combination/determination *Heliodilus soumagnei* Milne-Edwards, 1877; current determination *Tyto soumagnei* (A. Grandidier, 1878). Adult, sex undetermined, Toamasina type specimen.
- **Limits/design use:** taxonomy/name mapping and adult specimen identity only; it cannot support sex differences or juvenile development.

### Reuse, correction and unavailable material
- Reused `MRO-NHM-2003-P1112` for the separate approximately-30-day stage and `MRO-THORSTROM-NEST-1996` for the first-nest/fledging sequence. Those stages remain preserved in v4/v3 and are not merged into the age-unknown down-only Bemanevika nestlings.
- Corrected boundaries: a mean mass difference is not a visible height ratio; “covered only in down” does not license exact wing-pin counts; one documented deep tree cavity is not a universal nest template. New2 / reused2 / corrected3; scientific status false.
- Uploaded Mikkola guide was unavailable in this run, so no new page claim was made. Exact nestling age/sex, feather chronology and first locomotor competence remain unknown.

## 2026-11-05 — #62 *Ketupa sumatrana* v5 — Johor day-27 nestling boundary

### BEOW-FOLEY-2013-P34-37
- **Title / authors / year:** *Breeding Barred Eagle Owl Bubo sumatranus sumatranus in Johor, Malaysia* — Con Foley, Lau Weng Thor, Lau Jia Sheng & Tan Kok Hui, 2013.
- **URL:** https://www.orientalbirdclub.org/birdingasia-19 ; observer's complete field account: https://confoley.com/barred-eagle-owl/
- **Type / accessed:** primary 24/7 video/field study, *BirdingASIA* 19, printed pp.34–37; OBC contents lines 94–105 and the complete observer account read 2026-11-05.
- **Species/stage/pages:** historical *Bubo sumatranus sumatranus*; one Johor chick from estimated hatch through day-60 departure; printed pp.34–37.
- **Facts:** the monitored chick was still in its hatch position on day 27 but could stand upright and preen; it was no longer being continuously brooded. It began moving a few feet around the planter by day 34, ranged along the planter and flapped strongly by day 50, and departed at day 60 by a half-hop/half-flight to an overhanging branch. The urban resort planter contained soil and bougainvillea; adults avoided the nest when disturbed.
- **Limits/conflicts:** one pair and an estimated hatch date; the observer explicitly cautions that the reported adult plumage/size differences are sample size one. The unusual planter nest is a documented site, not a universal nest template. Day 27 posture does not support flight, branch movement or adult-proportioned remiges.
- **Design use:** day-27, soil/vegetation-supported upright-preening nestling at the original nest position; no flight, branch or precise cross-population feather claim. Adults remain near-equal rather than imposing one-pair dimorphism.
- **Links:** `research/species/62-ketupa-sumatrana-v5.md`; planned concept v5; run `production/runs/20261105T124400+0800-cycle-4.md`.

### Reused and corrected evidence
- Re-read `BEOW-LKC-SBR-2017-178` printed p.178 for the separate age-unknown pale transitional Pulau Ubin immature and `BEOW-SGBIRDS-2026` for current *Ketupa sumatrana* name/adult and broad juvenile identification.
- Corrected boundary: v5 does not transfer the 2017 presumed-subadult white tufts or the 2018 estimated 8–10-month hunting/flight behavior onto the day-27 Johor chick. New1 / re-read2 / corrected2; science false.

## 2026-11-05 — #55 *Bubo lacteus* v5 / current *Ketupa lactea* — restored source records and six-week boundary

### VEO-BROWN-1965-P101-104
- **Title / author / year:** *Observations on Verreaux's Eagle Owl Bubo lacteus (Temminck) in Kenya* — L. H. Brown, 1965.
- **URL:** https://zenodo.org/records/16138079/files/bhlpart371689.pdf?download=1
- **Type / accessed:** primary multi-year field observations, *Journal of the East Africa Natural History Society and National Museum* 25(2); opened 2026-11-05.
- **Species/stage/pages:** *Bubo lacteus*; nests and about-six-week nestling; printed pp.101–104, especially p.103; indexed lines 32–61.
- **Facts:** reuses abandoned/commandeered stick nests; young begin pale-grey-down, then fine grey/white barring; about six weeks, plain white thighs, black cheek streaks, almost-black eyes, pale blue-grey bill, yellowish-white feet and paler orange-pink lids; daytime crouch/stand and nocturnal walking/wing flapping; one left at 50–60 days.
- **Limits/conflicts:** small historical observation series, no measured feather plate or known hatch date for every bird; 50–60-day departure is not a universal flight threshold.
- **Design use:** fully supported approximately-six-week nestling in an old stick nest; no flight claim or adult-like wing pattern.

## 2026-11-05 — #52 *Strix hylophila* v5 — cavity-evidence correction

### RBO-COCKLE-ETAL-2024-P10
- **Title / authors / year:** *Ecology and conservation of cavity-nesting birds in the Neotropics: Recent advances, future directions, and contributions to ornithology* — Bonaparte, Cuatianquiz Lima, Ferreira-Xavier, da Hora, Di Sallo, López, Cockle & Núñez Montellano, 2024.
- **URL / DOI:** https://academic.oup.com/condor/article/126/4/duae042/7745762 ; https://doi.org/10.1093/ornithapp/duae042
- **Type / accessed:** peer-reviewed evidence review, *Ornithological Applications* 126(4):duae042; accessed 2026-11-05.
- **Scope / page:** Neotropical cavity-nesting evidence; *Strix hylophila* passage on article p.10 (indexed publisher PDF/HTML).
- **Facts:** after 18 years of Atlantic Forest nest-web work, the authors list *S. hylophila* among species that might use cavities but whose nests have not yet been confirmed or described; they warn against equating cavity approach/shelter with nesting.
- **Limit/conflict:** review, not a new nest description. It conflicts with secondary accounts that present high-tree-cavity nesting as settled.
- **Design use:** the v5 hollow is only a documented shelter/observation context; omit eggs, nest cup, lining and exact nest architecture; scientific status stays false.
- **Links:** `research/species/52-strix-hylophila-v5.md`; concept v5; run `production/runs/20261105T065000+0800-cycle-2.md`.

- Corrected two design claims: “confirmed high tree-cavity nest” → “single age-unknown chick observed in a hollow”; “dry wood-dust/shallow nest debris” → “plain natural hollow ledge without constructed nest material.” No source photograph is copied.

## 2026-11-04 — #201 *Otus mantananensis* v2 — nominate adults and fully grown-young boundary

### MANT-MARSHALL-1978-P14-15
- **Title / author / year:** *Systematics of Smaller Asian Night Birds Based on Voice* — Joe T. Marshall, 1978, Ornithological Monographs 25.
- **URL / accessed:** https://sora.unm.edu/sites/default/files/journals/om/om025.pdf ; accessed 2026-11-04.
- **Type / pages / scope:** field + museum systematic monograph, printed pp.14–15; nominate adults, habitat and older juvenile stage.

- **Design use:** adult structure/pattern, habitat, and branch-perching fully grown-young stage only.

## 2026-11-04 — #157 *Scotopelia bouvieri* v3 — riverine stick nest and down-rich young

### VFO-BROSSET-ERARD-1986-P63
- **Title / authors / year:** *Les oiseaux des régions forestières du Nord-Est du Gabon. I. Ecologie et comportement des espèces* — André Brosset & Christian Erard, 1986.
- **DOI / URL:** https://doi.org/10.3406/revec.1986.5179 ; https://www.persee.fr/doc/revec_1168-3651_1986_sup_41_3_5179?pageId=T1_63
- **Type / page / accessed:** primary long-term field account; species account navigated at Persée page view T1_63 (article pp.4–289); accessed 2026-11-04.
- **Facts read:** the species was associated with large rivers in NE Gabon; August nests were stick platforms in trees roughly 2.5–3 m above water, with only a few adult feathers; observed broods included one or two young around 40 days; another nest reused an old *Lampribis rara* nest; growth was unusually slow and one young estimated at 35 days still retained juvenile down about three months later.
- **Limits:** the account does not give a color plate, sexed adult comparison, exact 40-day feather map, eye chronology or locomotor threshold.
- **Design use:** retain an open stick platform above water; show a supported, down-rich circa-40-day young and make no flight claim.

### VFO-OISEAUXNET-2026-ID
- **Title / institution / accessed:** *Chouette pêcheuse de Bouvier — Scotopelia bouvieri* — Oiseaux.net; accessed 2026-11-04.
- **URL:** https://www.oiseaux.net/oiseaux/chouette.pecheuse.de.bouvier.html
- **Type / scope:** edited secondary species synthesis, adult identity and juvenile overview.
- **Facts read:** adults have dark eyes, a short yellowish bill, no ear tufts, brown upperparts with black vermiculation and pale scapular accents, pale sandy underparts with dark streaks, an indistinct facial disc, and bare tarsi/feet; the juvenile is described as pale mesoptile, sometimes with a cinnamon wash; listed length about 51 cm and species monotypic.
- **Limits:** secondary synthesis; no primary sample, exact age, sex-size effect or motion threshold. Juvenile color is used only as a cautious boundary alongside the primary down-retention record.
- **Design use:** calibrate adult vermiculation/soft parts and prevent the juvenile from becoming a miniature adult.

### VFO-NIKBORROW-2022-IMMATURE
- **Title / photographer / date:** *Vermiculated Fishing Owl Scotopelia bouvieri* — Nik Borrow; photographed 2022-08-24.
- **URL:** https://www.flickr.com/photos/nikborrow/52340778682/
- **Type / scope:** identified field photograph with locality metadata; immature at Akaka, Loango National Park, Gabon.
- **Facts/design:** supports a naturally perched older immature from Gabon and provides a real-bird comparison outside the nestling stage.
- **Limits:** no exact age, sex, growth sequence or nest context; used as a general plausibility check, not as a literal circa-40-day template.

### VFO-ITIS-555424-2026
- **Institution / accessed:** Integrated Taxonomic Information System; accessed 2026-11-04.
- **URL:** https://www.itis.gov/servlet/SingleRpt/SingleRpt?search_topic=TSN&search_value=555424
- **Type / scope:** official taxonomy database.
- **Fact:** accepts *Scotopelia bouvieri* Sharpe, 1875 as a species (TSN 555424).
- **Limits/design:** taxonomy only. The current inventory name matches the cited combination; no different historical combination was asserted without a read source.

### Reuse, correction and evidence boundary
- Existing v2 was inspected at original resolution. Its open stick nest is supported, not removed; the revision narrows the platform scale and corrects the juvenile from dark, adult-patterned fluff toward a pale down-rich supported stage.
- No quantified sex-size direction was found, so v3 uses near-equal adults and does not encode “female larger” as a rule.
- The user-uploaded Mikkola guide was not accessible in this run; no new page claim was taken from it.
- Links: `research/species/157-scotopelia-bouvieri-v3.md`; `production/runs/20261104T213333+0800-cycle-4.md`; planned concept `design/concepts/157-scotopelia-bouvieri-v3.png`.


## 2026-11-04 — #156 *Scotopelia peli* v3 — archive repair and downy nestling boundary

### PFOW-BUTTIKOFER-1889-P115
- **Title / author / year:** *Zoological researches in Liberia. On a series of birds collected by Mr. A. T. Demery in the district of Grand Cape Mount* — J. Büttikofer, 1889, Notes from the Leyden Museum XI.
- **URL / page:** https://repository.naturalis.nl/pub/509230/NLM1889011002006.pdf ; printed p.115.
- **Type / scope:** primary museum specimen note.
- **Fact read:** the collection included a nestling head in down; the down was uniform white with a faint isabelline tinge, especially along the neck, and the powerful bill was black. The same page treats *Scotopelia oustaleti* as synonymous with *S. peli*.
- **Limits:** head only; no age, iris, body feather map, wing development, movement or nest site. It supports the down color and bill, not an exact full-body reconstruction.

### PFOW-BIRDLIFE-SA-2025-ECOLOGY
- **Title / institution / accessed:** *Pel's Fishing Owl — Regional Red Data Book* — BirdLife South Africa; accessed 2026-11-04.
- **URL:** https://www.birdlife.org.za/red-data-book/red-list/pels-fishing-owl/
- **Type / scope:** institution-maintained conservation synthesis citing regional atlas and breeding sources.
- **Facts read:** river systems, pans and quiet backwaters with riparian vegetation and natural cavities; breeding in Botswana and northern South Africa peaks in the first half of the year; one chick is reared per attempt; fledglings later wander while adults are territorial.
- **Limits:** synthesis rather than the underlying breeding papers; no plumage sequence or exact nestling age.
- **Design use:** use a natural riverside cavity and one supported young; do not infer a detailed chronology.

### PFOW-SANBI-03164-2026
- **Title / institution / accessed:** *Scotopelia peli species status — diagnostics* — South African National Biodiversity Institute; accessed 2026-11-04.
- **URL:** https://speciesstatus.sanbi.org/taxa/detail/03164/
- **Type / scope:** institutional diagnostic and ecology synthesis.
- **Facts read:** about 63 cm and 2.14 kg; massive orange-rufous owl with round head, large dark eyes and no distinct facial disc; sexes similar, male deeper rufous and female slightly larger; dark spots/bars/transverse lines; large bare tarsi and powerful feet with spiculate soles; juveniles retain much pale white down for the first six months.
- **Limits:** cites Kemp (2005) and other sources; no measured sex sample, exact size ratio, age-specific feather map or iris chronology.
- **Design use:** restrain sex difference to subtle color/fullness and keep an older young strongly down-dominated.

### PFOW-NCBI-2599434-2026
- **Institution / accessed:** NCBI Taxonomy Browser; accessed 2026-11-04.
- **URL:** https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/Taxonomy/Browser/wwwtax.cgi?id=2599434
- **Type / scope:** institutional taxonomy database.
- **Facts:** current *Scotopelia peli* (Bonaparte, 1850); basionym *Strix peli* Bonaparte, 1850.
- **Limits:** NCBI states its taxonomy is not an authoritative nomenclatural source; no morphology or development.
- **Design use:** current/old-name mapping only. ITIS TSN 555422 independently returns the current combination in search results.

### Correction, reuse and evidence boundary
- Original-resolution inspection of archived v2 at commit `f316751d8e433eaf9db31c444c868ab5056cd39d` revealed a narrow title/upper-image strip and a blank gray remainder. V2 stays retained as evidence of the failure but cannot be the review master.
- Existing source-index entry referenced by v2 was not present in the current index text; this cycle adds new stable IDs instead of claiming reuse.
- Exact nestling age, full body feather map, iris, flight threshold and quantified sex ratio remain unknown. The display therefore uses an older age-unknown, fully cavity-supported downy nestling.
- The user-uploaded Mikkola guide was not accessible in this run; no page claim was taken from it.
- Links: `research/species/156-scotopelia-peli-v3.md`; `production/runs/20261104T214445+0800-cycle-5.md`; planned concept `design/concepts/156-scotopelia-peli-v3.png`.

## 2026-11-04 — #155 *Scotopelia ussheri* v3 — field-documented flying juvenile and adult separation

### RFO-MONTICELLI-KOROMA-BANNAH-2015-P183-188
- **Title / authors / year:** *Observations of Rufous Fishing Owl Scotopelia ussheri in Sierra Leone* — David Monticelli, Alusein P. Koroma & Denis Bannah, 2015.
- **URL:** https://www.researchgate.net/publication/281632427_Observations_of_Rufous_Fishing_Owl_Scotopelia_ussheri_in_Sierra_Leone
- **Type / accessed:** primary field observations with photographs; accessed 2026-11-04.
- **Scope / pages / figures:** adults and juvenile/immature; Bulletin of the African Bird Club 22(2), printed pp.183–188, especially pp.185–188 and Figs.1, 3–5.
- **Facts:** adult observed on raffia palm over a narrow slow river channel; upperparts are plain/mixed rufous, creamy-buff and white rather than uniformly finely barred; underparts finely dark-shaft streaked; bill bluish-grey with yellowish cere; unfeathered tarsi and toes yellowish-orange. The photographed juvenile was substantially smaller and stubbier, fluffier and slightly paler, had loose first-generation feathers, flew twice, and showed virtually adult-like uniformly dusky-barred rufous wings. Authors estimated 4–5 months if the assumed laying period is correct.
- **Limits / confidence:** high confidence for the photographed individual and behaviour; age is explicitly an inference, not a known hatch date; no sex-specific measurements and no nest description.
- **Design use:** center a smaller, stubby, pale-rufous, loose-feathered older juvenile with formed barred wings, natural two-foot branch grip and no helpless-chick pose; do not invent a nest. Adults remain near-equal because sex direction is unsupported.
- **Links:** `research/species/155-scotopelia-ussheri-v3.md`; concept v3; run `owl-pet/production/runs/20261104T220156+0800-cycle-6.md`.

### RFO-KOPPETSCH-BOHME-2022-P97-98
- **Title / authors / year:** *Juvenile Rufous Fishing Owl Scotopelia ussheri in Guinea* — Thore Koppetsch & Wolfgang Böhme, 2022.
- **URL:** https://www.researchgate.net/publication/359798544_Juvenile_Rufous_Fishing_Owl_Scotopelia_ussheri_in_Guinea
- **Type / accessed:** photographic historical record; accessed 2026-11-04.
- **Scope / pages / figures:** one captive juvenile photographed 6 October 1993 and again six months later; Bulletin of the African Bird Club 29(1), printed pp.97–98, Figs.1–3.
- **Facts:** first photographs showed whitish downy plumage; the same individual had adult plumage six months later. The paper distinguishes this young downy stage from Monticelli et al.'s free-living pale-rufous juvenile.
- **Limits / confidence:** exact age at capture, locomotor capacity and intervening feather chronology are unknown; captive setting is not nest evidence.
- **Design use:** cross-check that pale/whitish juvenile stages exist, but use the better-behaviour-documented Monticelli older juvenile for v3 rather than inventing a downy nestling.

### RFO-IUCN-BIRDLIFE-2021-ID-ECOLOGY
- **Title / institution / year:** *Scotopelia ussheri — Rufous Fishing-owl* — BirdLife International / IUCN Red List, assessed 2021.
- **URL / DOI:** https://doi.org/10.2305/IUCN.UK.2021-3.RLTS.T22689038A180213988.en
- **Type / accessed:** institutional assessment; accessed 2026-11-04.
- **Scope / section:** Identification; Habitat and Ecology.
- **Facts:** 46–51 cm, large orange owl, dark eyes, no ear tufts, uniform orangey-brown upperparts, paler buff finely streaked underparts; smaller and less uniformly barred above than *S. peli*. Preferred habitats are riverine rainforest and mangroves, including small shady streams and gallery forest; overhanging branches serve as fishing posts.
- **Limits / confidence:** synthesis rather than primary developmental research; no sex-specific size or juvenile chronology.
- **Design use:** adult silhouette/colour and shaded gallery-river setting; avoid Pel's Fishing Owl's uniformly barred upperparts.

### RFO-GBIF-COL-4VY2L-2026
- **Title / institution / accessed:** *Scotopelia ussheri* taxon record — GBIF / Catalogue of Life, accessed 2026-11-04.
- **URL:** https://www.gbif.org/taxon/4VY2L
- **Type / scope:** current taxonomic mapping.
- **Fact:** accepted *Scotopelia ussheri* Sharpe, 1871; equivalent historical combination *Bubo ussheri*.
- **Limits:** taxonomy only; no morphology or development.
- **Design use:** current/old-name mapping, not a visual source.

### Reused evidence, correction and unresolved items
- Reused existing v2 concept only as a style/composition reference; no existing dedicated #155 source entry was present in the current persistent index.
- Correction: v2's age-unknown whitish downy juvenile is not the strongest available action-capable stage. V3 uses the primary-paper's free-living, flying, estimated 4–5-month pale-rufous juvenile and explicitly keeps the age estimate uncertain.
- Still unknown: sex-specific size direction, exact hatch date, continuous feather sequence, universal first-flight timing, nest site/material, and how broadly one individual's wing pattern transfers.
- The uploaded Heimo Mikkola file was not accessible in this run; no new Mikkola page claim was made.


## 2026-11-04 — #13 *Micrathene whitneyi* v8 — quantitative 21–22-day feather boundary

### ELWO-LIGON-1968-UMMZ-P34-35
- **Title / author / year:** *The Biology of the Elf Owl, Micrathene whitneyi* — J. David Ligon, 1968.
- **Institution / source type:** University of Michigan Museum of Zoology, Miscellaneous Publications No. 136; original monograph.
- **URL / accessed:** https://deepblue.lib.umich.edu/bitstream/2027.42/56380/1/MP136.pdf ; accessed 2026-11-04.
- **Species / stage / pages:** *Micrathene whitneyi*; nestling development 0–28+ d; printed pp. 34–35, Figure 8.
- **Facts read:** nestling period 28–33 d; at 17–18 d body contour feathers are present, upper wing coverts exceed the exposed remiges, feet are almost bare, head down remains; at 21–22 d first flight attempts occur, tail is about 1.7 cm and mostly sheathed, and longest primary is 3.8 cm with 2.1 cm still sheathed; 25–26 d birds still fly ineffectively and capable flight appears around 27–28 d.
- **Limits / confidence:** high for the reported Arizona sequence and measurements; not a color plate and not proof of identical timing in every individual/subspecies.
- **Design / review use:** juvenile at approximately 21–22 d, completely supported, with retained head down, mostly contoured body, nearly bare feet, upper coverts prominent, short partly sheathed remiges and an extremely short mostly sheathed tail. Links: `research/species/13-micrathene-whitneyi-v8.md`; concept v8; run `production/runs/20261104T223131+0800-cycle-1.md`.

### ELWO-CORNELL-ID-2026
- **Institution / accessed:** Cornell Lab of Ornithology; accessed 2026-11-04.
- **URL / type:** https://www.allaboutbirds.org/guide/Elf_Owl/id ; institution-maintained identification account and photo reference.
- **Facts read:** tiny round-headed owl, no ear tufts, yellow eyes, white eyebrow, diffuse gray-brown/rufous/white spotting and streaking; juveniles are grayer and less buff/brown.
- **Limits:** secondary identification synthesis; no quantitative nestling sequence.
- **Design use:** adult identity and juvenile color-boundary cross-check only.

### ELWO-NPS-SAGUARO-2026
- **Institution / accessed:** National Park Service, Saguaro National Park; accessed 2026-11-04.
- **URL / type:** https://www.nps.gov/sagu/learn/nature/birds.htm ; institutional habitat and nesting summary.
- **Facts read:** Elf Owls use former woodpecker holes/cavities in trees and saguaros in Sonoran desert habitat.
- **Limits:** no detailed juvenile morphology or exact cavity geometry.
- **Design use:** saguaro-cavity context only.

### Reuse, correction and evidence boundary
- Reused the v7 Mikkola pp. 418–420 extract and ADW synthesis; the uploaded guide was not newly accessible in this run.
- Corrected the v7 evidence state: its `Ligon 1968` item was only an unverified lead. V8 read the original monograph and replaces that lead with `ELWO-LIGON-1968-UMMZ-P34-35`.

## 2026-11-04 — #17 *Megascops asio* v6 — first-days post-fledging support boundary

### EASO-SMITH-DEVINE-2003-P98-103
- **Title / authors / year:** “Eastern Screech-Owl Nesting Cycle: Care and Feeding of Young” — Dwight G. Smith and Arnold Devine, 2003.
- **Source / URL / pages:** *Connecticut Warbler* 23(3); https://ctbirding.org/wp-content/uploads/2017/01/CTWarblerVolume23.pdf ; printed pp. 98–103; accessed 2026-11-04; primary field observations from 16 active nests.
- **Facts read:** regional fledging chronology; in the first days after fledging young perched in nest/nearby trees; first flights were short and landings poor or misjudged, followed by scrambling, clinging and climbing to upright perch; flight improved within days.
- **Limits:** Connecticut sample plus literature comparisons; no fine feather plate or exact age for each pictured bird.
- **Design use:** newly fledged, fully branch-supported juvenile using both feet and a half-open balancing wing after a poor landing; formed but softer/less orderly juvenile remiges are allowed. Links: `research/species/17-megascops-asio-v6.md`; run `production/runs/20261104T225412+0800-cycle-2.md`.

### EASO-CORNELL-LIFE-2026
- **Institution / URL / accessed:** Cornell Lab; https://www.allaboutbirds.org/guide/Eastern_Screech-Owl/lifehistory ; accessed 2026-11-04.
- **Facts read:** 26–30 d nestling period; young may first hop to ground or branches and climb with feet plus fluttering wings; cavity/nest-box placement.
- **Limits:** institutional secondary synthesis, no quantitative feather map.
- **Design use:** action, support and cavity context.

### EASO-CORNELL-ID-2026
- **Institution / URL / accessed:** Cornell Lab; https://www.allaboutbirds.org/guide/Eastern_Screech-Owl/id ; accessed 2026-11-04.
- **Facts read:** northern juveniles fluffy gray and finely barred; adult size/shape, ear tufts, yellow eyes and gray/rufous morph context.
- **Limits:** photo-identification account; no exact juvenile age.
- **Design use:** adult identity and juvenile surface pattern.

### EASO-USFS-FEIS-1995
- **Institution / DOI / accessed:** USDA Forest Service FEIS; 10.2737/feis-species-review-meas; accessed 2026-11-04.
- **Facts read:** current *Megascops asio*, synonym *Otus asio*; eyes normally open by day 20, flight feathers begin around day 25, nest stay 4–5 weeks.
- **Limits:** 1995 secondary synthesis.
- **Design use:** taxonomy and timing cross-check.

### EASO-USGS-KOZLOWSKI-HAHN-2010
- **Authors / source:** Kozlowski & Hahn 2010, *Wilson Journal of Ornithology* 122:755–761; USGS record https://pubs.usgs.gov/publication/70003571.
- **Facts read:** primary developmental study included 22–28 d nestlings.
- **Limits:** hormone study; no plumage inference.
- **Design use:** stage sampling context only.

### Reuse and correction
- Reused v5 Mikkola printed pp. 207–209 for adult and mesoptile traits; upload not newly reopened.
- Corrected v5's approximate 27–30 d “brancher before competent flight” into a first-days post-fledging field-observation boundary: formed wing permitted, but poor landing, clinging and full branch support are mandatory.
- Source accounting: new verified 5; reused 1; corrected design boundary 1.


## 2026-11-04 — #10 *Surnia ulula* v4 — 20–22-day nest departure without inferred flight confidence

### NHO-KERTELL-1986-JRR-P91-101
- **Title / author / year:** “Reproductive Biology of Northern Hawk-Owls in Denali National Park, Alaska” — Kenneth Kertell, 1986.
- **Source / URL / pages:** *Raptor Research* 20(3/4):91–101; original PDF https://sora.unm.edu/sites/default/files/journals/jrr/v020n03-04/p00091-p00101.pdf ; printed pp. 91–101; accessed 2026-11-04.
- **Species / population / stage:** *Surnia ulula* in Denali, Alaska; two 1980 nesting pairs plus supplementary fledgling observations.
- **Facts read:** printed p.92 reports adult barring differences between sexed pair members but explicitly notes they may be age-related; pp.96–97 describe hollow broken-top white-spruce nests on decomposed sapwood, white closed-eye young at an estimated first week, and nest departure at approximately 20–22 d.
- **Limits / confidence:** primary field evidence, high for the observed Denali nests; hatching age was estimated, sample small, no continuous feather plate, and adult color difference is not secure sexual dimorphism.
- **Design / review use:** use near-equal same-morph adults; show a newly nest-departed 20–22-day youngster fully supported on a broken-spruce surface; do not infer confident flight from nest departure. Links: `research/species/10-surnia-ulula-v4.md`; run `production/runs/20261104T231133+0800-cycle-3.md`.

### NHO-NPS-DENALI-2021-2026
- **Institution / URL / accessed:** U.S. National Park Service, Denali; https://home.nps.gov/articles/northern-hawk-owl.htm ; article dated 2021, accessed 2026-11-04.
- **Facts read:** in Alaska young leave at about three weeks and can walk, climb, hop and make short horizontal flight bursts but cannot fly long distances; hollow broken trees/cavities are typical; adults are long-tailed, pointed-winged, yellow-eyed and female averages are only modestly larger.
- **Limits:** institutional synthesis rather than a feather chronology; “about three weeks” is not an exact individual age.
- **Design use:** supported locomotor boundary and conservative female fullness; no long-distance or confident flight pose.

### NHO-EFAUNA-BC-2026
- **Institution / author / URL / accessed:** E-Fauna BC, species account by Jamie Fenneman; https://linnet.geog.ubc.ca/efauna/Atlas/Atlas.aspx?noTransfer=1&sciname=Surnia+ulula ; accessed 2026-11-04.
- **Facts read:** adult dark gray-brown upperparts with white scapular/wing spots, long narrowly barred tail, heavily barred white underparts, bordered pale face, yellow iris/bill and feathered feet; at 2–3 weeks young gain gray mesoptile plus adult-like flight and tail feathers, with nest departure around 23–27 d; young crown is gray and largely unspotted, facial disc dark/poorly defined and barring faint.
- **Limits:** curated secondary account; timing differs slightly from Kertell's estimated 20–22 d Denali boundary and does not provide a measured feather map.
- **Design use:** surface plumage and formed-but-not-adult-crisp wing/tail boundary; retain uncertainty instead of forcing one universal day.

### Reuse, correction and evidence boundary
- Reused v3 Mikkola printed pp.360–363 and Huhtala, Korpimäki & Pulliainen 1987 growth-study citation. The uploaded Mikkola file was not newly accessible.
- Corrected the design statement “pre-flight 24–27-day branch-clamberer” to a more conservative Denali first-departure boundary: approximately 20–22 d, fully supported, locomotion-capable, with formed juvenile flight/tail feathers but no confident-flight claim.
- Source accounting: new verified 3; reused 2; corrected design boundary 1. Exact individual age, continuous feather sequence, flight threshold, subspecies and universal nest form remain unknown.
