- **Title / institution / date:** “Eastern Barn Owl” — Australian Museum; updated 29 July 2022.
- **URL / type / accessed:** https://australian.museum/learn/animals/birds/eastern-barn-owl/ ; institutional identification page, opened 2026-11-06.
- **Facts:** white heart disc; sandy-orange/light-grey above; white/cream below; black spotting; young similar; females described qualitatively as slightly larger.
- **Limits/design use:** overview, not quantified sexing evidence; combined with ABSA overlap data, adults remain near-equal in visible height.

### JAV-ABSA-BIRD-IN-HAND-2E-P1
- **Title / institution:** “Eastern Barn Owl — Bird in the Hand (Second Edition)” — Australian Bird Study Association Inc., compiled by J.W. Hardy from HANZAB/BirdLife Australia.
- **URL / type / pages / accessed:** https://absa.asn.au/wp-content/uploads/2017/04/Eastern-Barn-Owl.pdf ; one-page banding guide, full PDF read 2026-11-06.
- **Facts:** male/female wing, tail and mass ranges overlap; text states no significant measurement differences. Males tend paler, females darker/buffer; flecks vary, with females tending larger/more leg and underwing-coverts flecks. Australian subspecies ageing data are incomplete.
- **Conflict/design use:** corrects a rigid “female larger” rendering. Use near-equal height, slight qualitative colour/spotting contrast only; no reliable visual sexing claim.

## 2026-11-06 — #92 *Ninox japonica* v3 — age, sex-size and recent-fledgling boundary

### JAP-FUKAI-2016-PP22-28
- **Title/author/year:** “Age determination of Brown Hawk-Owl (*Ninox scutulata*)” — Norio Fukai, 2016, *Bulletin of the Japanese Bird Banding Association* 28:22–28.
- **URL/DOI/accessed:** https://www.jstage.jst.go.jp/article/jbba/28/1/28_MS073/_pdf ; full PDF read 2026-11-06.
- **Pages/species/stages:** printed pp.22–26; Japanese *N. s. japonica*, juvenile, first-winter and adult specimens/captures/photos.
- **Facts:** external sexing and size-only sexing unreliable because plumage and measurements overlap; juvenile rectrices taper and carry a broad pale terminal fringe comparable with inner pale bands, while adults have broader rounded rectrices and a narrow whiter tip; juvenile body/covert molt begins before flight feathers finish growing.
- **Limits/design use:** tail character supports a formed but juvenile-finished recent fledgling; it does not supply exact day, flight ability or continuous tract chronology. High confidence; older name maps to current *N. japonica*.
- **Links:** `research/species/92-ninox-japonica-v3.md`; v3 concept/review; `production/runs/20261106T073000+0800-cycle-1.md`.

- **Title/authors/year:** “Breeding ecology of the Northern Boobook *Ninox japonica totogo* in central Taiwan” — Wen-Loung Lin, Si-Min Lin & Hui-Yun Tseng, 2012, *Forktail* 28:164–166.
- **URL/accessed:** https://web.ntnu.edu.tw/~treehopper/lib/publications_lib_pdf/nonox_et_al_2012.pdf ; full author-hosted PDF read 2026-11-06.
- **Pages/species/stages:** printed pp.164–166, Tables 1–3; breeding adults, nestlings and nest sites of resident Taiwan *totogo*.
- **Facts:** ten tree-cavity nests; 25–28 day nestling period; no significant male–female difference in measured bill, head, wing, tail, tarsus or mass. Paper explicitly maps the old *N. scutulata* complex to split *N. japonica* and contrasts *japonica* and *totogo*.
- **Limits/design use:** Taiwan population cannot establish a precise Japanese pair ratio. Supports near-equal visible adult scale and no universal female-larger template. High confidence.
- **Links:** same #92 v3 records.

- **Institution/URL/section:** The Owl Pages / Deane Lewis; https://www.owlpages.com/owls/species.php?s=1730 ; lines 64–75; re-read 2026-11-06.

- **Conflict excluded:** its generic cavity/egg/incubation paragraph is probabilistic and is not used against the 2018 “nest undescribed” statement.
- **Linked decision:** `research/species/107-strix-fulvescens-v5.md`; planned v5 darkens only adult irises. New2 / re-read1 / reused2+ / corrected2; science false.

## 2026-11-06 — #113 Ninox natalis v7 evidence refresh

### CIB-DCCEEW-HILL-2004
- Title: National Recovery Plan for the Christmas Island Hawk-Owl Ninox natalis
- Author/institution/year: Richard Hill / Birds Australia for Australian Government Department of the Environment and Heritage / 2004
- URL: https://www.dcceew.gov.au/sites/default/files/documents/n-natalis.pdf
- Accessed: 2026-11-06
- Type: government recovery plan synthesising field studies
- Species/stage/topic: Ninox natalis; adult habitat, nesting, independent-young evidence gap
- Pages/sections actually read: printed pp.1–3 (PDF pp.5–7), Taxonomic status, Habitat, Habitat Critical to Survival
- Facts: full species after Norman et al.; primary/marginal rainforest; four recorded nests were tree hollows, all in Syzygium nervosum; no data on independent-young ranging.
- Limits/conflict: recovery synthesis, not a feather-development series; modern hollow record supersedes using the 1900 screw-pine/twig report as definitive.
- Design support: rainforest and tree-hollow ecology; conservative branch support; no precise juvenile action claim.

### CIB-WAM-JOHNSTONE-2016
- Title: Information Sheet — Christmas Island Hawk-Owl Ninox natalis
- Author/institution/year: Ron Johnstone, Department of Terrestrial Vertebrates, Western Australian Museum / 2016

- Accessed: 2026-11-06
- Type: museum institutional species account with photographs
- Pages actually read: English p.1
- Facts: 26–29 cm; rufous-brown; small white upper-wing-covert spots; darker barred wing/tail; white lores/short supercilium/chin; yellow eyes, blue-grey bill, pale-yellow legs/feet; female described as slightly larger; juvenile paler/downier.
- Limits/conflict: no measurements or exact juvenile age; “female slightly larger” is not converted to a fixed ratio and is checked against overlapping historical wing ranges.

### CIB-ANDREWS-1900
- Title: A Monograph of Christmas Island (Indian Ocean)
- Author/institution/year: C. W. Andrews / British Museum / 1900
- URL: https://flow.hemiptera-databases.org/flowpdf/484.pdf
- Accessed: 2026-11-06
- Type: historical primary specimen/field monograph
- Pages actually read: printed pp.47–48 (PDF extraction lines 1193–1204)
- Facts: sexes alike in colour; male wing 7.6–7.8 in and female 7.4–7.8 in overlap; iris/feet bright lemon-yellow.
- Limits/conflict: historical screw-pine/twig nest was not personally found and conflicts with modern four-hollow record; retained as uncertainty, not used as design.

- Image/caption actually read/viewed: image 4 of 4; caption, pixel metadata and actual displayed image
- Facts visible: rufous face/yellow iris, loose pale down on head/body, formed short rounded wing with irregular small pale markings, branch support.
- Limits: exact age, sex, tail length and flight capacity absent; pixels do not provide a feather chronology.

- Title: Molecular Data Confirms the Species Status of the Christmas Island Hawk-Owl Ninox natalis

## 2026-11-06 — #120 *Megascops ingens* v4 — primary-paper reread and juvenile-wing correction

### RIS-ORDONEZ-2022-LEAD
- **Title/authors/year/type:** “Primer registro del polluelo y nuevos datos del nido del Autillo Rojizo (*Megascops ingens*)…” — L. Ordóñez-Delgado, F. Ramon Castillo & A. Pineda Díaz, 2022, *Neotropical Biodiversity* 8(1):301–304; primary field report.
- **DOI/URL/accessed:** https://doi.org/10.1080/23766808.2022.2123729 ; author-uploaded full text at https://www.researchgate.net/publication/364265788 ; reread 2026-11-06.
- **Pages/figures actually read:** printed pp.301–304, especially p.302 and Fig.1; repository landing page and displayed full-text extraction.
- **Facts/design use:** one southeast-Ecuador chick, age estimated rather than known at 20–30 d; light-beige base, dark-brown face/eye contour, whitish forehead, faint brown bands and residual white natal down; primaries/secondaries/tertials already considerably developed and barred light/dark brown; gray bill with pale-yellow egg-tooth vestige, dark-brown iris; perched at a simple bare dead-stump cavity rim in lower-montane evergreen forest edge. The observed threat response included slight wing opening and bill-clapping, not demonstrated flight.
- **Limits/conflict:** single chick and one observed nest; no hatch date, sustained-flight test, sex or continuous feather tract sequence. Supports a visibly formed but folded, bounded juvenile flight-feather cluster—not wing buds and not an adult-perfect wing.
- **Links:** `owl-pet/research/species/120-megascops-ingens-v4.md`; planned v4 concept/review; `owl-pet/production/runs/20261106T151000+0800-cycle-5.md`.

### RIS-ITIS-686668-2026
- **Institution/URL/section:** ITIS TSN 686668, https://www.itis.gov/servlet/SingleRpt/SingleRpt?search_topic=TSN&search_value=686668 ; taxonomy/nomenclature and direct children; read 2026-11-06.
- **Facts/use:** valid *Megascops ingens*; original synonym *Scops ingens*; current species contains listed subspecies including nominate *M. i. ingens*. Taxonomy only.

### RIS-ITIS-686872-2026
- **Institution/URL/section:** ITIS TSN 686872, https://www.itis.gov/servlet/SingleRpt/SingleRpt?print_version=PRT&search_topic=TSN&search_value=686872&source=to_print ; taxonomy/nomenclature; read 2026-11-06.
- **Facts/use:** valid nominate *M. i. ingens*; historical combinations *Otus ingens* and *Megascops albogularis aequatorialis*. Taxonomy/locality mapping only.

### Reuse and corrected boundary
- Reused `RIS-MIKKOLA-2013-P229` from the archived #120 v3 record (printed p.229 / uploaded PDF p.231): small adult tufts, honey-brown eyes, olive-yellow bill/cere, 25–28 cm, 134–223 g and females averaging 28 g heavier. The upload was not accessible in this execution, so this is explicitly a repository-pinned reuse rather than a fresh page claim.
- New/corrected sources: 2 taxonomy records; primary field paper reread: 1; repository source reused: 1. V3 visually underplays the paper's “considerably developed” barred remiges, so v4 must enlarge the juvenile's formed folded flight-feather cluster while retaining down, no tufts and full support. Exact age, flight ability, literal feather map and fixed sex-size ratio remain unknown; science=false.

## 2026-11-06 — #123 *Otus gurneyi* v5 — corrected photographed juvenile wing boundary

### GSO-SHANKAR-2013-YOUNG-CORR — corrected full-resolution juvenile photograph record
- Shankar S., “Giant scops owl juvenile,” Davao, Philippines, May 2013; Wikimedia Commons transfer from Flickr, CC BY 2.0: https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Giant_scops_owl_juvenile_(9105615728).jpg
- File description, metadata and original 4608×3072 JPEG actually opened. The supported age-unknown older juvenile has loose gray-brown down, short broad fuzzy ear tufts, dark bill/eyes, pink-purple eyelid margins and narrow dark formed flight-feather side panels; the visible right panel approaches branch/foot level while the tail is not projected. This corrects v4's over-concealment of formed side feathers. Exact age, sex, hatch plumage, wild nest and sustained flight remain unknown; captive Davao context cannot establish natural nest habitat. High confidence for visible morphology, low for developmental timing.

### GSO-MIRANDA-2011 — newly indexed primary phylogeny and museum measurements
- Hector C. Miranda Jr., Daniel M. Brooks & Robert S. Kennedy, 2011, “Phylogeny and Taxonomic Review of Philippine Lowland Scops Owls (Strigiformes),” *Wilson Journal of Ornithology* 123(3):441–453, DOI 10.1676/10-185.1: https://blog.hmns.org/wp-content/uploads/2023/10/PhilippineScopsEprint.pdf
- Full paper actually read. Printed p.446, Table 2 reports *Mimizuku gurneyi* male wing range 213–230 mm (n=5) and female 242–285 mm (n=2); this supports only a cautious female-larger direction, not a fixed ratio. Printed p.451 proposes *Otus gurneyi* because separate-genus status is not phylogenetically warranted. High confidence for taxonomy/measurements; no juvenile development support.

### GSO-OWLPAGES-2020 — reused secondary habitat/breeding boundary
- Deane Lewis, Owl Pages, “Giant Scops Owl (*Otus gurneyi*),” updated 2020-10-02: https://www.owlpages.com/owls/species.php?s=290
- Description, Size, Breeding and Habitat sections read. Useful only for a generic lowland rainforest/secondary-growth context and the explicit statement that breeding biology is virtually unknown. It cannot support a nest, natal stage or exact sex ratio. Moderate confidence secondary source.

## 2026-11-06 — #126 *Strix hadorami* v3 — taxon and c35-day fledgling boundary

### DESO-KIRWAN-2015 — newly indexed primary taxonomic description
- Guy M. Kirwan, Manuel Schweizer & José Luis Copete, 2015, “Multiple lines of evidence confirm that Hume's Owl *Strix butleri* … is two species, with description of an unnamed species,” *Zootaxa* 3904(1):28–50. DOI: https://doi.org/10.11646/zootaxa.3904.1.2 ; author PDF: https://nnhc.huji.ac.il/sites/default/files/nnhc/files/15-zootaxa-kirwan_schweizer_copete_compressed.pdf
- Accessed 2026-11-06; primary taxonomy/museum study. Actually read pp.28, 36–41 and 43, Diagnosis, holotype, specimen and variation sections. Supports western *S. hadorami* boundary; pale grey-white disc; sandy-buff/cinnamon-grey mottling; light-rufous underpart cross-barring; no pronounced sex dimorphism, with females only slightly larger on average. It does not set a pair-specific display ratio or juvenile stage. High confidence for adult identity/taxonomy.
- Mappings: *Strix hadorami*; historical western *Strix butleri*; adult morphology; sex scale. Applied to `research/species/126-strix-hadorami-v3.md` and v3 review.

### DESO-BENDOV-2017 — newly indexed primary breeding/development study
- Amir Ben Dov, Aviam Atar, Erez Baruchi, Kochav Levi & Nir Sapir, 2017, “Breeding biology of Desert Owl in Israel,” *Dutch Birding* 39:285–307. Full text: https://www.researchgate.net/publication/320517643_Breeding_Biology_of_Desert_Owl_in_Israel ; institutional record: https://cris.haifa.ac.il/en/publications/breeding-biology-of-desert-owl-in-israel/
- Accessed 2026-11-06; primary field study. Actually read pp.285–289, 292–301, Table 4 and plate captions 392, 394–398, 406–421. Directly supports rock/nest-shelf fledglings, observed first flight at estimated c30 days, mobile c35-day fledglings and fresh breast feathers at c70–75 days. The authors explicitly say development evidence was limited and partly calibrated with Tawny Owl; age is estimated, exact hatch date/iris/feather map and continuous flight competence are not proven. High confidence for support/habitat and moderate for estimated stage.
- Mappings: *Strix hadorami*; c35-day fledgling; rock ledge; residual down plus formed wing; behaviour/development. Applied to `research/species/126-strix-hadorami-v3.md` and v3 review.


## 2026-11-06 — #133 *Megascops atricapilla* v4 — manifest-chain repair

### BCSO-DANTAS-2021 — newly indexed primary taxonomy
- Sidnei M. Dantas, Jason D. Weckstein, John Bates, Joiciane N. Oliveira, Therese A. Catanach & Alexandre Aleixo, 2021, “Multi-character taxonomic review, systematics, and biogeography of the Black-capped/Tawny-bellied Screech Owl (*Megascops atricapilla–M. watsonii*) complex,” *Zootaxa* 4949(3):401–444. DOI: https://doi.org/10.11646/zootaxa.4949.3.1
- Accessed 2026-11-06. Primary taxonomy/morphometrics. Journal article and abstract read; the project-linked printed p.429 species treatment was cross-checked. The study used 252 specimens, reports high plumage variation, and did not detect significant morphometric separation among complex clades. It supports the current Atlantic-Forest unit and cautions against diagnosing the unit from a single rigid plumage pattern. It does not establish a universal sex-height ratio or juvenile chronology.
- Mappings: *Megascops atricapilla*; historical *Strix atricapilla* / *Otus atricapilla*; adult variation. Applied to `research/species/133-megascops-atricapilla-v4.md`.

### BCSO-WIKIAVES-WA2019052 — newly indexed direct young record
- Celso B. Almeida, WA2019052, photographed 2016-02-07, Florianópolis/SC; https://www.wikiaves.com.br/2019052
- Accessed 2026-11-06. Reliable field-photo record with metadata “Sexo: Indeterminado” and “Idade: Jovem.” Displayed image supports yellow iris, tiny tufts, soft head/face, formed short wing/tail and bilateral branch support. It does not supply exact age, sex, fledging status, flight ability or a continuous plumage sequence; image is linked, not copied.
- Mappings: *Megascops atricapilla*; later juvenile; direct support. Applied to v4.

### BCSO-NATURALIS-TYPES-2023 — newly indexed nomenclatural primary catalogue
- Steven D. van der Mije, Pepijn Kamminga & René W. R. J. Dekker, 2023, “Type specimens of non-passerines in Naturalis Biodiversity Center (Animalia, Aves),” *ZooKeys* 1155:1–311. DOI: https://doi.org/10.3897/zookeys.1155.98097
- Accessed 2026-11-06; printed pp.182–183 read. The catalogue maps *Strix atricapilla* Temminck, 1822 to *Megascops atricapilla* and identifies NMW 40468 as the original single Natterer specimen. Later RMNH specimens received under *Otus decussatus* do not have type status. High confidence for name/type provenance only; no morphology, sex or development support.
- Mappings: current/old name, type provenance. Applied to v4 name mapping.


## 2026-11-06 — #134 *Megascops sanctaecatarinae* v4 — manifest-chain repair

### LTSC-PAGANO-2021 — newly indexed primary natural-history synthesis
- Luis G. Pagano et al., 2021, “Distribución e historia natural del Alilicucú Orejudo o Ribereño (*Megascops sanctaecatarinae*) en Argentina,” *El Hornero* 36(2):51–64. DOI: https://doi.org/10.56178/eh.v36i2.370
- Accessed 2026-11-06; article abstract, identification/habitat statements and references read. Forty-one records from 18 Misiones localities; voice is key, body size/breast scaling can help, while tuft length and iris colour are not diagnostic. Stream-associated records came from degraded/recovering forest or forest strips in this dataset.
- Limitation: not a juvenile development series or fixed sex-height formula. Applied to `research/species/134-megascops-sanctaecatarinae-v4.md`.

### LTSC-KACHIMARECK-2014-WA1527488 — newly indexed direct chick record
- José Kachimareck, WA1527488, photographed 2014-10-08, Curitiba/PR; https://www.wikiaves.com.br/1527488
- Accessed 2026-11-06. Record metadata explicitly gives sex indeterminate and age “Filhote”; displayed image supports charcoal-gray soft upper body, fine diffuse dark marks, weak head wisps, dark-appearing eyes, open greenish-horn bill and cavity support.
- Limitation: lower wings/tail/feet, exact age, iris colour, fledging status and flight ability are unseen/unknown. Image linked, not copied.

### LTSC-ITIS-686677 — newly indexed taxonomy mapping
- Integrated Taxonomic Information System, TSN 686677, accessed 2026-11-06: https://www.itis.gov/servlet/SingleRpt/SingleRpt?print_version=PRT&search_topic=TSN&search_value=686677&source=to_print
- Current valid *Megascops sanctaecatarinae*; synonyms *Otus sanctaecatarinae* and *Scops sanctaecatarinae*; English Long-tufted Screech Owl. High confidence for names only; no morphology or development support.


### GHO-HOFFMEISTER-SETZER-1947
- **Title / author / year:** The Postnatal Development of Two Broods of Great Horned Owls / Donald F. Hoffmeister & Henry W. Setzer / 1947
- **URL:** https://www.gutenberg.org/cache/epub/35118/pg35118-images.html
- **Accessed / type:** 2026-11-06 / primary longitudinal nest-development study, University of Kansas Museum
- **Species / stage:** *Bubo virginianus*; hatch to 49-day young
- **Useful evidence:** pp.165–166; daily-observed young left at 43–49 d, one 43-d bird lacked coordinated flight, feather tables show substantial erupted remiges by late nestling stage, and immature down remained relevant.
- **Limits / confidence:** four young, local Kansas nests, historic study; high for observed individuals and stage bounds, not a universal continuous schedule.
- **Decision links:** #56 v2 c.44–46 d supported brancher; substantial irregular down-veiled folded wing, no sustained-flight pose. Research/review: `research/species/56-bubo-virginianus-v2.md`.

### GHO-MCGILLIVRAY-1989
- **Title / author / year:** Geographic Variation in Size and Reverse Size Dimorphism of the Great Horned Owl in North America / W. Bruce McGillivray / 1989
- **DOI / URL:** 10.2307/1368060 / https://academic.oup.com/condor/article-abstract/91/4/777/5189226
- **Accessed / type:** 2026-11-06 / peer-reviewed specimen study; abstract read
- **Species / stage:** *Bubo virginianus* adults; 418 specimens, 18 variables
- **Useful evidence:** reverse size dimorphism virtually constant among regions, while absolute size varied geographically/subspecifically.
- **Limits / confidence:** only abstract accessible; does not supply one plate-ready pair ratio. High for direction, bounded for depiction magnitude.
- **Decision links:** moderately fuller/slightly taller female, no fixed numeric ratio or subspecies claim in #56 v2.

### GHO-CORNELL-CAM-2022
- **Title / institution / date:** Great Horned Owl Cam Timeline / Cornell Lab Bird Cams / 2022 timeline
- **URL:** https://www.allaboutbirds.org/cams/great-horned-owl-cam-timeline/
- **Accessed / type:** 2026-11-06 / institutional direct camera chronology
- **Species / stage:** *Bubo virginianus*, known-age branching/fledging
- **Useful evidence:** one chick branched at 46 d and made short limb-to-limb flight at 55 d; institutional summary says 6-week birds branch/climb and 7-week birds make tiring short flights.
- **Limits / confidence:** one camera individual plus institutional generalization; high for that individual, moderate for broad timing.
- **Decision links:** supported brancher, no airborne pose in #56 v2.

- **Limits / confidence:** taxonomy only; no plumage/development evidence.
- **Decision links:** name mapping for #56 v2.

- **URL:** https://www.neotropicalbirdclub.org/cotinga/C39_online/Kirkconnell%20%26%20Wiley.pdf
- **Accessed / type:** 2026-11-06 / primary multi-year field survey, *Cotinga* 39:10–23.
- **Species / stage:** *Margarobyas lawrencii*; breeding pairs and active cavities, 2006–12.
- **Useful evidence:** printed pp.11, 13–15, Tables 1–2; Bare-legged Owl was recorded breeding in active cavities at Bermeja and Mera-Molina in seasonally inundated forest with sabal palm savanna. Loss of standing cavity-bearing palms reduced available nest sites.
- **Limits / confidence:** population/cavity-habitat evidence, not a nestling morphology or sex-size study; palm cavity is locally supported, not universal. High for the surveyed sites.
- **Decision links:** #116 v4 uses a natural standing-palm cavity entrance and no constructed nest; exact cavity geometry remains unknown. Applied to `research/species/116-margarobyas-lawrencii-v4.md`.

### BLO-SUAREZ-2022-CATALOGUE
- **Title / author / year:** *Catalogue of Cuban fossil and subfossil birds* / William Suárez / 2022.
- **DOI / URL:** https://doi.org/10.25226/bboc.v142i1.2022.a3 / https://bioone.org/journals/bulletin-of-the-british-ornithologists-club/volume-142/issue-1/bboc.v142i1.2022.a3
- **Accessed / type:** 2026-11-06 / peer-reviewed museum catalogue and comparative osteology review.
- **Species / stage:** *Margarobyas lawrencii*; modern/fossil adults, taxonomic history.
- **Useful evidence:** printed pp.59–60; comparisons included >30 modern individuals, documented broad size/osteological and external variation, treated western `exsul` as invalid, and mapped historical `Gymnoglaux` material to *Margarobyas lawrencii*.
- **Limits / confidence:** no living sexed body-size table or juvenile feather sequence; does not justify a plate-ready male/female ratio. High for name mapping and variability boundary.
- **Decision links:** v4 keeps adults near-equal and does not assign a rigid subspecies morph or universal female-larger rule.

## 2026-11-12 — #124 *Ketupa philippensis* v5 — age-label correction and institutional visual cross-check

### PEO-MACAULAY-ML624492559

- **Stable ID / title:** PEO-MACAULAY-ML624492559 — “ML624492559 — Philippine Eagle-Owl.”
- **Creator / institution / date:** Adrian Constantino / Birding Philippines; Macaulay Library, Cornell Lab of Ornithology; media date not supplied; accessed 2026-11-11.
- **URL / source type:** https://macaulaylibrary.org/asset/624492559 — institutional media catalogue and full image; species-specific visual reference.
- **Taxon / stage / locality:** *Ketupa philippensis*; age and sex explicitly “Not specified”; National Capital Region (Manila), Philippines.
- **Actually read / viewed:** catalogue lines 53–99 and the linked 2048×1536 image. The record supplies contributor, Manila locality, explicit unknown age/sex and low-light capture metadata (ISO 3200, 1/80 s, no flash). It is useful as a cautious current visual cross-check only.
- **Limits / confidence:** high confidence for record metadata, low for sex, age, calibrated colour and subspecies diagnosis. It must not support a sex-size rule, juvenile chronology or iris-colour chronology.
- **Design / QA decision:** reinforces the rule that an unaged photograph must not be silently assigned as male, female or juvenile. V5 therefore keeps adult body size near equal and labels the young “OLDER JUVENILE — AGE UNKNOWN,” while juvenile iris colour remains an explicit inference rather than a fact.
- **Links:** `research/species/124-ketupa-philippensis-v5.md`; `design/concepts/124-ketupa-philippensis-v5.png`; `research/reviews/124-ketupa-philippensis-v5-review.md`.

### Re-read and boundary correction

- `PEO-URRIZA-2017` author-posted abstract was re-read at lines 26–31: weekly observations over five weeks and “at least 70 days to fledge” are a minimum for one young, not a day-age label or feather-sequence chart.
- `PEO-OCHAVO-2012` was re-read at lines 48–70: the juvenile was observed flapping on 3 January, left the structure on 7 January and made a long flight on 14 January. The sequence supports an older supported young and wing exercise, but supplies no plumage chronology or exact age.
- `PEO-ITIS-1244715-2026` is reused for the current-name and old-name mapping. `PEO-MIK2013-P294` is reused only from the repository-pinned printed pp.294–295 because the uploaded guide was not mounted in this execution.
- **Correction applied:** v4’s visible label “NEAR-FLEDGED” was more specific than the displayed individual’s evidence. V5 uses “OLDER JUVENILE — AGE UNKNOWN”; it may show a compact folded wing and a supported balancing lean, but no flight, spread wing, exact age or adult-perfect covert rows. Science remains false.

Anz. 53:54–93; especially pp.54–55, 59–63, 68–69, 74–76 and Figs.2,5,11,13), DAV-MIK2013-348 (uploaded PDF pp.350–351 / printed pp.348–349), DAV-SCHERZINGER-FANG-2006, DAV-FANG-ETAL-2009. The primary 2014 paper overrides Mikkola’s bold concentric-face depiction and the unsupported “always much larger” female wording; it directly supports the about-four-week nest-box young. Full design boundary: `research/species/132-strix-davidi-v4.md`. Science remains false.


## 2026-11-06 — #135 *Otus silvicola* v4 — direct guide conflict and nomenclature

### WSC-MIK2013-183 — new
- **Title / author / year:** *Owls of the World: A Photographic Guide*, 2nd ed. / Heimo Mikkola / 2013.
- **Location actually read:** printed p.183; searchable text lines 2972–2977.
- **Species / stages:** *Otus silvicola*; adult and juvenile.
- **Evidence:** no data on sexual size differences; long tufts without white inner borders; no pale collar; cold grey-brown upperparts; sparse bold ventral streaks/cross-bars; dull orange-yellow eyes; greyish-horn bill/cere; densely feathered tarsi/toe bases. Juvenile paler/fluffier, more reddish-brown and less distinctly patterned.
- **Limits:** secondary synthesis; no exact juvenile age, flight threshold or continuous feather sequence. Its “no data” statement conflicts with over-generalizing Mees’s one-pair size direction.
- **Decision:** v4 uses pose asymmetry but no fixed female-larger ratio; fully feathered transitional immature is bounded by Mees.

- **Institution / record:** Integrated Taxonomic Information System, TSN 555386; accessed 2026-11-06.
- **URL:** https://www.itis.gov/servlet/SingleRpt/SingleRpt?print_version=PRT&search_topic=TSN&search_value=555386&source=to_print
- **Evidence:** current valid *Otus silvicola*; synonym/original combination *Scops silvicola*.

**Reused and freshly reread:** `WSC-MEES-2006` (printed pp.113–115, especially p.114), `WSC-MARSHALL-1978`, `SLSO-SANGSTER-2013`. Full design boundary and prompt: `research/species/135-otus-silvicola-v4.md`. Science remains false.

## 2026-11-07 — #137 *Otus alfredi* v4 — montane-habitat and juvenile-specimen calibration

### FLO-HUTCHINSON-2007
- **Title / authors / year:** “Observations of Flores Scops Owl *Otus alfredi* on Flores, Indonesia” — R. Hutchinson, J.A. Eaton, B. Demeulemeester & D.R.C. Buzzetti, 2007.
- **URL / type / pages / accessed:** https://static1.squarespace.com/static/5c1a9e03f407b482a158da87/t/5c20f94f4d7a9c6cf87abdf2/1545664849106/Hutchinson-FloresScops.pdf ; primary field observations, *Forktail* 23:184–187; printed pp.184–187; accessed 2026-11-06 UTC.
- **Species / stages / facts:** current *Otus alfredi*; adults small and compact with short rounded tufts, solid-rufous unrimmed face, distinct white brows, yellow eyes/legs/feet, finely vermiculated lower flanks and belly without black streaks, solid-rufous upperparts, localized white scapular marks and small well-spaced pale primary notches. Records principally in primary montane forest; one roadside encounter involved remnant large trees beside scrub/tall grass.
- **Limits / confidence:** no dated juvenile sequence or quantified sexual dimorphism; high confidence for adult field appearance and habitat, not for young.
- **Design decision:** retain near-equal adults and primary montane forest; do not invent sex differences or juvenile timing.
- **Links:** `research/species/137-otus-alfredi-v4.md`; planned `design/concepts/137-otus-alfredi-v4.png`.

### FLO-WIDODO-1999 — reused and page-calibrated
- **Title / authors / year:** “Rediscovery of the Flores Scops Owl *Otus alfredi* on Flores, Indonesia, and reaffirmation of its specific status” — W. Widodo, J.H. Cox & P.C. Rasmussen, 1999.
- **URL / type / pages / accessed:** https://static1.squarespace.com/static/5c1a9e03f407b482a158da87/t/5c1fc23b2b6a282c991de675/1545585212532/Widodo-Flores.pdf ; peer-reviewed museum/field study, *Forktail* 15:15–23; Table 1, Table 2, Figs.1–2 and printed pp.15–23; re-read 2026-11-06 UTC.
- **Species / names / facts:** current *Otus alfredi*; protonym *Pisorhina alfredi*. Adults 192–207 mm total length; MZB juvenile 185 mm total length. The age-unknown 1994 juvenile from 1,400 m wet montane forest retained slightly paler nearly uniform rufous natal down across neck, mantle, wing coverts, scapulars and uppertail coverts, with a few pale shaft streaks and clearer tail banding. Historical type-series sex assignments are uncertain.
- **Limits / confidence:** one juvenile cannot establish age, flight threshold, iris colour or general sex-size rule; high confidence for that specimen’s visible plumage and measurements.
- **Design decision:** depict a smaller, bilaterally supported, down-retaining, compact-winged age-unknown juvenile; adults remain near equal.
- **Links:** `research/species/137-otus-alfredi-v4.md`; previous `research/species/137-otus-alfredi-v3.md`.

### FLO-BIRDLIFE-FACTSHEET-2026 — unverified lead
- **Institution / URL / accessed:** BirdLife International DataZone; https://datazone.birdlife.org/species/factsheet/flores-scops-owl-otus-alfredi ; accessed 2026-11-06 UTC.
- **Status:** dynamic full text was not readable in this execution; retained only as a range/status lead. It is not used for morphology, sex, age, behaviour or the science pass.


## 2026-11-07 — #138 *Otus mentawi* v4 — complete-image recovery and juvenile-stage recheck

### MEN-TAUFIQURRAHMAN-2018-P31-32 — newly indexed primary field-development record
- **Title / authors / year:** “First breeding record of Mentawai Scops Owl *Otus mentawi*, with notes on the morphology and vocalisations of juveniles”, Imam Taufiqurrahman, Ismael Saumanuk & Arif Setiawan, 2018.
- **URL / access:** https://swaraowa.org/wp-content/uploads/2018/05/mentawai-scops-owl.pdf ; accessed 2026-11-07.
- **Type / pages / scope:** primary field observation; BirdingASIA 29, printed pp.31–32; west Siberut adults and two dated, branch-using juveniles observed 12–21 May 2017.
- **Useful source facts:** the adult usually called high while a juvenile called from a lower branch, sometimes 2–3 m above ground; by 18 May two juveniles were slightly smaller than the adult. They had pale greyish-brown underparts with thin dark streaks, darker upperparts, reddish-brown faces and eyes, pale greyish-horn bills/toes, short dark tails, and folded wings projecting beyond the tail with faint narrow bars that strengthened by 21 May.
- **Limits / confidence:** exact hatch dates were not known; the paper’s roughly 25-day incubation/fledging chronology is explicitly comparative and only a reasonable estimate. It does not document sex-specific standing-size ratio or continuous flight ability. High confidence for the photographed dated plumage/support stage.
- **Design decision:** use the later documented juvenile stage on a natural lower branch with both feet supported; retain formed but non-adult wings beyond a short tail, and do not label exact age or flight competence.
- **Links:** `research/species/138-otus-mentawi-v4.md`; target `design/concepts/138-otus-mentawi-v4.png`.

### MEN-MIKKOLA-2013-P166-167 — newly re-read uploaded guide account
- **Title / author / year:** *Owls of the World: A Photographic Guide*, 2nd ed., Heimo Mikkola, 2013.
- **Library file / access:** user-uploaded PDF `libfile_363b879863d48191adc5747cea29008e`; read 2026-11-07.
- **Type / pages / scope:** photographic species account; PDF pages 168–169 / printed pp.166–167; adult *Otus mentawi*.
- **Useful source facts:** length about 20 cm, wing 157–166 mm; no data on sexual size differences; rufous adults have dark reddish-brown upperparts, no nuchal collar, a whitish-and-black scapular row, mottled dark-brown wing coverts, reddish-brown/chestnut underparts with herringbone marks and white ocelli, brown (sometimes yellow) eyes, yellowish-horn bill, fully feathered tarsi and bare flesh-coloured toes with powerful dark claws. Lowland rainforest plus secondary growth/villages are within habitat.
- **Limits / confidence:** the guide said juvenile unknown before the 2018 field paper; one photographic account cannot define every individual palette. High confidence for adult identity and the absence of quantified sex-size direction.
- **Design decision:** keep adults near equal apparent size, select brown eyes and yellowish-horn bills, feather tarsi to the toe bases, and use village-edge lowland rainforest without inventing a nest.
- **Links:** `research/species/138-otus-mentawi-v4.md`; v1–v3 retained.

### MEN-ITIS-555383-2026 — newly indexed current/old-name crosswalk
- **Institution / access:** Integrated Taxonomic Information System, TSN 555383; accessed 2026-11-07.
- **URL:** https://www.itis.gov/servlet/SingleRpt/SingleRpt?search_topic=TSN&search_value=555383
- **Type / scope:** authoritative taxonomy record; species-level nomenclature.
- **Useful source facts:** valid current name *Otus mentawi* Chasen & Kloss, 1926; listed synonym *Otus bakkamoena mentawi* Chasen & Kloss, 1926.
- **Limits / confidence:** taxonomy only; not used for plumage, sex, age, behaviour or nest claims. High confidence for this name mapping.
- **Design decision:** retain roster label *Otus mentawi* and record the historical trinomial without changing the 251-species scope.
- **Links:** `knowledge/species/138-otus-mentawi.md`; `research/species/138-otus-mentawi-v4.md`.

### MEN-CHASEN-KLOSS-1926-DOI — metadata-only historical lead
- **Citation:** Chasen & Kloss, 1926, “Spolia Mentawiensis.—Birds”, *Ibis* 68:269–306. DOI: https://doi.org/10.1111/j.1474-919X.1926.tb07583.x
- **Verification state:** publisher metadata and page range read 2026-11-07; full original species text was not available in this run. Do not use this entry as direct morphology evidence.


## 2026-11-07 — #139 *Otus jolandae* v4 source delta

### RIN-SANGSTER-2013-P8-11 — verified primary description
- **Title / authors / year:** “A New Owl Species of the Genus *Otus* (Aves: Strigidae) from Lombok, Indonesia”, George Sangster, Ben F. King, Philippe Verbelen & Colin R. Trainor, 2013.
- **URL / DOI:** https://doi.org/10.1371/journal.pone.0053712 ; open full text: https://journals.plos.org/plosone/article?id=10.1371/journal.pone.0053712
- **Access / type / pages:** read 2026-11-07; peer-reviewed open primary species description, pp.8–11 and diagnosis/holotype sections; species and one specimen retaining juvenile feathers.
- **Useful source facts:** current species was separated from the Lombok population long treated as *O. magicus albiventris*; adults are dark cinnamon-brown and less boldly patterned, with warm cinnamon breast and paler finely barred belly; holotype label records golden-yellow iris, brown bill and dull-ochre feet. Female AMNH 629942 retained scattered white to buffy-white juvenile feathers with brown spots/bars on crown and nape. The paper documents degraded/secondary forest and elevations 25–1350 m.
- **Limits / confidence:** seven historical specimens and field adult photographs; the retained juvenile feathers occur on one female specimen and do not define a complete juvenile stage, exact age, iris chronology, flight ability or population sex-size relation. High confidence for diagnosis and historical-name context; limited for development.
- **Design decision:** render an age-unknown older immature, not a chick: smaller/fluffier, formed but softened wing and short tail, with only scattered pale retained crown/nape feathers. Keep adults comparable in displayed scale and avoid repeated bright covert spots.
- **Links:** `research/species/139-otus-jolandae-v4.md`; target `design/concepts/139-otus-jolandae-v4.png`.

### RIN-MIKKOLA-2013-P188 — re-read uploaded photographic guide
- **Title / author / year:** *Owls of the World: A Photographic Guide*, 2nd ed., Heimo Mikkola, 2013.
- **Library file / access:** user-uploaded PDF `libfile_363b879863d48191adc5747cea29008e`; read 2026-11-07.
- **Type / pages / scope:** photographic species account; PDF p.190 / printed p.188; adult *Otus jolandae*.
- **Useful source facts:** about 23 cm, 115 g, wing 148–157 mm; no sexual-size data; relatively long ear tufts, warm-brown upperparts, whitish scapular band, golden-yellow eyes, blackish-brown bill, rusty underparts with irregular pale/rich-brown marks, legs feathered to dull-ochre toe bases; forested and degraded habitats to 1350 m.
- **Limits / confidence:** explicitly says juvenile undescribed; pre-publication photographic synthesis, not a developmental study. High for adult/habitat boundary, none for a complete juvenile atlas.
- **Design decision:** preserve adult soft parts and comparable display size; the older immature must be constrained by the one primary specimen cue rather than inferred from the related *O. magicus* juvenile account on the next page.
- **Links:** `research/species/139-otus-jolandae-v4.md`; previous v1–v3 retained.

### RIN-ITIS-1063240-2026 — current-name verification
- **Institution / access:** Integrated Taxonomic Information System, TSN 1063240; accessed 2026-11-07.
- **URL:** https://www.itis.gov/servlet/SingleRpt/SingleRpt?search_topic=TSN&search_value=1063240
- **Type / scope:** authoritative taxonomy record; species-level current standing.
- **Useful source facts:** *Otus jolandae* Sangster, B. F. King, Verbelen & Trainor, 2013 is a valid species; English common name Rinjani Scops Owl.
- **Historical-name note:** the primary description documents former assignment of the Lombok material to *Pisorhina albiventris*, *Scops albiventris* and *Otus magicus albiventris*; these are historical specimen assignments, not ITIS-listed synonyms.
- **Limits / confidence:** taxonomy only; not used for morphology, sex, development, behaviour or nest claims.
- **Design decision:** retain roster name *Otus jolandae* and record the historical assignments without altering the 251-species scope.

## 2026-11-07 — #141 *Otus magicus* v4 research delta

### MAG-MIKKOLA-2013-P189-190 — uploaded guide directly re-read
- **Title / author / year:** *Owls of the World: A Photographic Guide*, 2nd ed. / Heimo Mikkola / 2013.
- **Locator:** printed pp.189–190; user-uploaded PDF pp.191–192; directly read 2026-11-07.
- **Species/stage:** *Otus magicus*, including northern-Moluccan *O. m. leucospilus*; adult and older feathered juvenile.
- **Facts:** no sexual-size data; extensive grey-brown to rufous morph variation; yellow adult iris; pale greyish-black bill/cere; strong feet with distal tarsus bare 6–9 mm; juvenile described as fluffy/adult-like with narrow dark-brown crossbars on head, crown and neck and fainter spots/freckles. The leucospilus account is paler than nominate birds.
- **Limit:** no exact juvenile day age, iris, flight ability, bill colour, quantified sex ratio, nest construction or continuous feather chronology.
- **Design use:** adults displayed near equal size; older supported juvenile with short soft folded wing and unknown iris not asserted; no nest claim.

### MAG-SHARPE-1875-P70-PLVI — historical primary catalogue and plate
- **Title / author / year:** *Catalogue of the Birds in the British Museum*, vol. 2 / R. B. Sharpe / 1875.
- **URL:** https://www.biodiversitylibrary.org/page/34374448
- **Locator/read:** p.70 and Plate VI, directly read 2026-11-07.
- **Species/stage:** historical *Scops magicus* / *Scops leucospilus* material; adult female description and historical adult/juvenile plate.
- **Facts:** adult female described as dark sandy buff with strong irregular barring; barred wing and tail are visible in the account/plate.
- **Limit:** historical artwork is not a dated field-development sequence and cannot establish exact juvenile age, flight capacity or iris.
- **Design use:** broad barred-pattern comparator only; juvenile stage remains age unknown.

### MAG-SANGSTER-2013 — museum-based complex boundary, re-read
- **Title / authors / year:** “A new owl species of the genus *Otus* from Lombok, Indonesia” / Sangster, King, Verbelen & Trainor / 2013.
- **DOI / URL:** https://doi.org/10.1371/journal.pone.0053745 ; https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC3564762/
- **Locator/read:** specimen comparisons and taxonomic discussion (pp.8–11 in article PDF), directly re-read 2026-11-07.
- **Species/stage:** *Otus magicus* complex adults; Lombok population boundary.
- **Facts:** museum comparison of 115 specimens; Lombok birds formerly included in broad *O. magicus* are treated as *O. jolandae*. Adult pattern differences support keeping northern-Moluccan leucospilus separate from the Lombok concept.
- **Limit:** not a developmental study and does not fill chick/juvenile chronology.
- **Design use:** exclude Lombok *O. jolandae* traits from #141.

### MAG-ITIS-555379-2026 — current and original-name mapping
- **Title / institution:** ITIS Report — *Otus magicus*, TSN 555379 / Integrated Taxonomic Information System.
- **URL:** https://itis.gov/servlet/SingleRpt/SingleRpt?search_topic=TSN&search_value=555379
- **Accessed/read:** taxonomy, nomenclature and direct children, 2026-11-07.
- **Facts:** valid *Otus magicus* (S. Müller, 1841); synonym/original combination *Strix magica* S. Müller, 1841; *O. m. leucospilus* listed among direct subspecies. Current English name Moluccan Scops Owl.
- **Limit/use:** nomenclature and subspecies mapping only; no morphology or development.

### MAG-LODGE-YOUNG-LEAD-2026 — unverified/excluded lead
- Search located a lodge page captioned “young Moluccan Scops Owl,” but location/context appear to concern southern/Flores material and the source is non-institutional. The underlying age, taxon and image provenance were not verified. It is excluded from v4 design evidence and retained only as a future lead.

**Run synthesis:** four source records added to the central index (three biological/taxonomic sources plus one uploaded-guide section); Mikkola and Sangster are evidence reused from v3 but directly re-read. Exact juvenile age/iris/flight/bill, sex-size ratio, nest form and full feather chronology remain unknown. Scientific verification remains false.


## 2026-11-07 — #142 *Asio grammicus* v3 research delta

### JAM-MIKKOLA-2013-P500-501 — uploaded guide directly re-read
- **Title / author / year:** *Owls of the World: A Photographic Guide*, 2nd ed. / Heimo Mikkola / 2013.
- **Locator:** printed pp.500–501; uploaded PDF pp.502–503; directly read 2026-11-07.
- **Species/stage:** historical *Pseudoscops grammicus*, current *Asio grammicus*; adult, white first down, older juvenile.
- **Facts:** adult length 27–34 cm, wing 197–229 mm; no known sexual size/colour difference; long mottled brown-black tufts; warm tawny-brown upperparts without white scapular spots; barred flight feathers/tail; dull yellowish underparts with long dark shaft streaks and fine brown vermiculation; hazel-brown eyes; bluish-grey bill/cere; reddish-brown tarsal feathering to toe bases; grey-brown toes and dark claws. Juvenile is lighter above with pale grey-brown back and dull cinnamon-buff remainder; a July caption notes down remnants on the head.
- **Limits/use:** no exact juvenile age, flight threshold, iris chronology, sex-size direction, nest construction or continuous feather sequence. Supports an age-unknown, supported down-remnant juvenile, not a hatchling.

**Run synthesis:** six central-index records added (four directly read/re-opened this run; two preserved prior reads), one unverified lead retained but excluded from design. Exact juvenile age/iris/flight/sequence, nest form, and sex-size direction remain unknown; scientific verification remains false.


## 2026-11-07 — #143 *Asio solomonensis* v3 — juvenile source reread and current-name mapping

### FEO-LOYN-DEBUS-2000-P288-289
- **Title/authors/year:** “Description of Juvenile Fearful Owl *Nesasio solomonensis*” — Richard H. Loyn & S. J. S. Debus, 2000, *Australian Bird Watcher* 18(7):288–289.
- **URL/accessed/type:** https://afo.birdlife.org.au/afo/index.php/afo/article/download/215/205 ; accessed 2026-11-07; primary field paper and photographs.
- **Pages/figure actually read:** printed pp.288–289; Plate 79 and caption.
- **Facts/design use:** the Bougainville young was lively, walked and bounced on the ground and perched on railings; it had long unbarred down on head and underparts, a dark facial mask, emerging barred light/dark-brown dorsal and wing feathers, orange-yellow irides, blue-grey cere, black bill and grey feet. The only reported natal site was a hollow tree in lowland forest.
- **Limits/conflict:** a single rescued bird of unknown age and sex; no flight test or full feather sequence. The railing is documented temporary care context, not a universal nest. High confidence for visible juvenile morphology and supported locomotion; low for chronology/generalization.

## 2026-11-07 — #145 *Ninox philippensis* v3 — adult diagnosis, taxon mapping and constrained fledgling reuse

### LZBO-RASMUSSEN-2012-P13-14
- **Title/authors/year:** “Vocal divergence and new species in the Philippine hawk owl *Ninox philippensis* complex” — Pamela C. Rasmussen et al., 2012, *Forktail* 28:1–20.
- **URL/accessed/type:** https://www.asianscientist.com/wp-content/uploads/2012/08/Vocal-divergence-and-new-species.pdf ; publication landing https://www.orientalbirdclub.org/forktail28/ ; accessed 2026-11-07; peer-reviewed primary taxonomic/morphology paper.
- **Pages/figure actually read:** printed pp.13–14; Fig.6a adult from Dolores, Quezon, Luzon.
- **Facts/design use:** small compact sleek boobook; small buff-white supercilium; plain dark auriculars with filamentous extensions; poorly defined disc; uniform brown crown-rump without barring/speckling; large white/pale-buff dark-edged scapular patch; one/two large white outer-covert spots without barring; weak narrow pale tail bands; narrow white finely streaked throat; prominent narrow elongated brown/rufescent ventral streaks; yellow iris with paler outer ring, pale olive-yellow cere/bill, yellow tarsi/toes and mostly black claws.
- **Taxon scope:** philippensis/proxima/centralis/ticaoensis treated as one species; subspecific divisions weak and Siquijor centralis retained pending further study.
- **Limits/conflict:** individual attribution can be problematic and the paper does not provide a juvenile chronology or fixed sex-size display ratio. High confidence for adult diagnosis and concept; none for ageing.

### LZBO-ITIS-555484-2026
- **Institution/title:** Integrated Taxonomic Information System — *Ninox philippensis*, TSN 555484.
- **URL/accessed/type:** https://itis.gov/servlet/SingleRpt/SingleRpt?print_version=PRT&search_topic=TSN&search_value=555484&source=to_print ; accessed 2026-11-07; authoritative taxonomy database.
- **Section actually read:** nomenclature, common names and direct children.
- **Facts/design use:** valid *Ninox philippensis*; common names Luzon Boobook, Philippine Hawk-Owl and Luzon Hawk-Owl; direct subspecies *centralis*, *philippensis* and *ticaoensis*.
- **Limits:** taxonomy only; no morphology, sex or development inference.

### LZBO-CLEMENTS-2013-SPLIT
- **Institution/title/year:** Cornell Lab of Ornithology, Clements Checklist August 2013 updates and corrections.
- **URL/accessed/type:** https://www.birds.cornell.edu/clementschecklist/introduction/updateindex/overview-august-2013/updates-and-corrections-august-2013/ ; accessed 2026-11-07; official checklist update.
- **Section actually read:** Philippine Hawk-Owl complex split entry.
- **Facts/design use:** records the split into five species and retains philippensis/ticaoensis/centralis within Luzon Boobook *N. philippensis*. Supports current-concept mapping.
- **Limits:** checklist taxonomy/range mapping only; no morphology or development.

### LZBO-MIKKOLA-2013-P473 — reused repository evidence
- **Title/author/year:** *Owls of the World: A Photographic Guide*, 2nd ed. — Heimo Mikkola, 2013.
- **Locator:** printed p.473 / uploaded PDF p.475, as extracted in the archived #145 v2 research record.

## 2026-11-07 — #146 *Ninox mindorensis* v3 — historic adult pair evidence and juvenile misreading guard

### MINDBOO-MCGREGOR-1904-P16-17
- **Author/title/year:** Richard C. McGregor, *Bulletin of the Philippine Museum* no.3, “On birds from Luzon, Mindoro, Masbate, Ticao, Cuyo, Culion, Cagayan Sulu, and Palawan,” 1904.
- **URL/accessed/type:** https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/5/50/Bulletin_of_the_Philippine_Museum_%28IA_acx3431.0001.001.umich.edu%29.pdf ; accessed 2026-11-07; primary museum specimen account.
- **Pages/lines actually read:** printed pp.16–17; scan text lines 5166–5252.
- **Specimen/facts:** adult male no.1767, Puerto Galera, Mindoro, 2 Dec 1902; narrowly buff-barred head/neck, broad dark throat shaft stripes, white lateral throat areas, tawny-buff narrowly barred underparts, white undertail coverts, large white scapular/greater-covert spots, dark tail with about eight narrow buff bars, hair-tipped decomposed auricular feathers; wing 6.30 in, tail 3.50 in, tarsus 1.19 in, culmen 0.76 in. Comparison with Grant’s female type says the sexes are similar.
- **Critical limit:** the author initially mistook this confirmed adult male for a young bird because it differed from *N. philippensis*. That sentence is not juvenile evidence and must not be used to invent a developmental stage.
- **Design use/confidence:** high for the described historic male and near-equal sex direction; no juvenile chronology, iris, flight or feather sequence.
- **Links:** `owl-pet/research/species/146-ninox-mindorensis-v3.md`; target v3 concept.

### Reuse and search result
- Reused: `LZBO-RASMUSSEN-2012-LEAD`, `MINDBOO-ITIS-2025`, `MINDBOO-CLEMENTS-2013` from the v2 record.
- Searches under current name, historical *N. philippensis mindorensis* and specimen terms found no verified species-specific juvenile account. The juvenile remains an explicitly age-unknown conservative placeholder; science false.
- User-uploaded Mikkola guide was inaccessible in this execution; no fresh page claim.

## 2026-11-07 — #161 *Ketupa blakistoni* v3 — Hokkaido scope, late-nestling stage and 2026 split

### BLF-BIRDRESEARCH-HAYASHI-2010-P1-4 — newly re-read institutional ecological guide
- **Title / author / institution / year:** “Blakiston’s Fish Owl”; Yoshihiro Hayashi / Japan Bird Research Association, *Bird Research News* 7(2), 2010.
- **URL:** https://www.bird-research.jp/1_shiryo/seitai/shimafukuro.pdf
- **Accessed / type:** 2026-11-07; institutional species guide citing field work and literature; full indexed text actually read. Screenshot rendering of PDF pp.3–4 failed after text retrieval and is recorded as a tooling limit.
- **Pages / stage:** printed pp.1–4; p.3 breeding and nesting; p.4 growth/dispersal; Photo 2 caption identifies a nestling at about 50 days.
- **Facts:** riparian broadleaf/mixed forest beside rivers, lakes, marshes and seashore; ice-free water is important. Female broods while male provisions. Natural nests are chiefly cavities in very large broadleaf trees; nest boxes are also used, with rare kite-nest/cliff-ledge cases. Incubation 35–38 days; usual fledging 48–60 days, one 69-day report; young are normally fed until autumn and sometimes winter.
- **Design/check use:** choose the directly documented ~50-day late nestling, low and fully supported at a large-tree cavity threshold; do not claim flight. Use Hokkaido riverine old-growth rather than a generic nest bowl.
- **Limits/confidence:** high for quoted guide facts and the pictured/captioned stage; synthesis rather than a single controlled development series. It does not provide a feather-tract timetable or individual flight test.

### BLF-HAYASHI-1997-283-285 — newly indexed primary telemetry note
- **Title / author / year:** “Home Range, Habitat Use and Natal Dispersal of Blakiston’s Fish-Owls”; Yoshihiro Hayashi, 1997, *Journal of Raptor Research* 31(3):283–285.
- **URL:** https://sora.unm.edu/sites/default/files/journals/jrr/v031n03/p00283-p00285.pdf
- **Accessed / type:** 2026-11-07; primary field/radio-telemetry paper; pp.283–285 actually read.
- **Facts:** Hokkaido population depends on riparian forest; fledglings had been colour-marked since 1987; one radio-tagged male fledgling remained in its natal area for about a year before dispersal.
- **Design/check use:** supports an extended post-fledging dependence context and strict riparian setting, but not plumage reconstruction.
- **Limits/confidence:** high for tracked individuals; no exact plumage, sex-dimorphism or 50-day locomotor description.

### BLF-CLEMENTS-2026-SPLIT — newly indexed current taxonomy change
- **Title / institution / date:** “2026 Taxonomy Update”; Clements/eBird taxonomy, Cornell Lab of Ornithology, October 2026.
- **URL:** https://www.birds.cornell.edu/clementschecklist/updates-corrections-october-2026/
- **Accessed / type:** 2026-11-07; current institutional checklist update, section “Northern Fish-Owl”.
- **Facts:** Northern Fish-Owl *Ketupa doerriesi* is split from Blakiston’s Fish-Owl *K. blakistoni*. The retained *K. blakistoni* is monotypic and restricted to Hokkaido/northern Japan and adjacent insular Russia; continental northeast Asian birds are *K. doerriesi*. The update cites vocal and plumage differences including a white nape patch and tail pattern.
- **Design/check use:** v3 is explicitly Hokkaido *K. blakistoni* sensu stricto and must not borrow the conspicuous white nape/back patch or near-white tail of continental *doerriesi*. Historical broad-sense *Bubo/Ketupa blakistoni* references are scope-checked before transfer.
- **Limits/confidence:** high for current Clements/eBird treatment; other authorities may update on different schedules. This naming correction does not itself establish development.

### Reuse and evidence boundary
- Reused repository records **BLF-MIKKOLA-2013-P296**, **BLF-OWLPAGES-2020**, **BLF-MOVIN-2022** and **BLF-IOC13-1-2023** only within their previously indexed limits. The user-uploaded Mikkola guide was not mounted in this execution, so no fresh page claim is made.
- Adult plumage is alike; measured mass direction supports a fuller/heavier female but not a fixed standing-height ratio. Exact 50-day flight ability, continuous feather chronology, individual feather map and universal sex ratio remain unknown.
- Full prompt: [PROMPTS_FULL.md](../production/PROMPTS_FULL.md#161-ketupa-blakistoni-v3-2026-11-07). Research card: [161 v3](species/161-ketupa-blakistoni-v3.md). Scientific verification remains false.


## 2026-11-07 — #162 *Aegolius acadicus* v2 — juvenal plumage and sex-ratio correction

### NSWO-MCCULLOUGH-CONWAY-2017
- **Title / authors / year:** “Breeding behavior of Northern Saw-whet Owls in Oregon” — McCullough & Conway, 2017.
- **URL / DOI / accessed:** https://doi.org/10.3955/046.091.0211 ; USGS publication record and abstract read 2026-11-07.
- **Type / species / stage:** primary nest-camera study; *A. acadicus*, incubation and nestling.
- **Facts / location:** 858 h video from two artificial nest boxes; one successful nest; accessible abstract reports 30-day incubation and nestling periods and stage-dependent female attendance/prey delivery.
- **Limits / design use:** one successful nest; supports “late cavity nestling,” not an exact pictured age or universal schedule.
- **Links:** `research/species/162-aegolius-acadicus-v2.md`; v2 prompt/review.

### NSWO-PROJECT-OWLNET-HY
- **Title / institution / accessed:** “Hatching Year” — Project Owlnet; read 2026-11-07, section “Hatching Year Northern Saw-whet Owls.”
- **URL:** https://www.projectowlnet.org/hatching-year
- **Type / species / stage:** institutional field-ageing guide; juvenal plumage through first prebasic molt.
- **Facts:** near-fledging young acquire fulvous/chocolate juvenal plumage in the cavity; juvenal flight feathers are retained while body plumage changes later.
- **Limits / design use:** not a controlled growth series; supports a formed short juvenal wing without adult-pattern spot rows or a flight claim.
- **Links:** #162 v2 research, prompt and review.

### NSWO-CORNELL-GALLERY
- **Title / institution / accessed:** Northern Saw-whet Owl photo gallery — Cornell Lab of Ornithology, All About Birds; juvenile/adult captions and images read 2026-11-07.
- **URL:** https://www.allaboutbirds.org/guide/Northern_Saw-whet_Owl/photo-gallery/
- **Type / species / stage:** curated field photographs; adults and juveniles.
- **Facts:** juvenile references show cinnamon belly, plain dark brown back and bold pale facial V; adult references show white crown streaking and white-spotted brown upperparts.
- **Limits / design use:** selected photographs with variable locality/age; supports pattern separation, not exact growth timing.
- **Links:** #162 v2 research and review.

### NSWO-PAXTON-WATTS-2008
- **Title / authors / year:** “Mass variation in Northern Saw-whet Owls” — Paxton & Watts, 2008.
- **URL / DOI / pages / accessed:** https://doi.org/10.1111/j.1557-9263.2008.00145.x ; *Journal of Field Ornithology* 79(1):53–57; abstract/results read 2026-11-07.
- **Type / species / stage:** primary recapture study; adults/older birds, sex classification.
- **Facts:** recaptures (n=372) showed variable mass and instability in mass-based sex assignment; wing-chord thresholds overlap.
- **Limits / design use:** does not establish appearance of one individual; rejects a fixed female/male display ratio.
- **Links:** #162 v2 research and prompt.

### NSWO-PRIESTLEY-ETAL-2026
- **Title / authors / year:** “Classifying the Sex of Northern Saw-whet Owls Using Morphometrics” — Priestley et al., 2026.
- **URL / DOI / accessed:** https://doi.org/10.3356/jrr2573 ; *Journal of Raptor Research* 60(3), abstract/results read 2026-11-07.
- **Type / species / stage:** primary DNA-sexed morphometric study; autumn migrants.
- **Facts:** Alberta sample (n=445) found average female mass/wing chord above male means and a 93% model; authors caution that regional calibration is required.
- **Limits / design use:** overlapping distributions and migrant sample; only a subtle fuller female, never deterministic enlargement.
- **Links:** #162 v2 research, prompt and review.


## 2026-11-07 — #163 *Glaucidium gnoma* v3 — species-limit boundary and supported mesoptile

### GNOMA-DESHLER-2020-RSD-ABSTRACT — newly read abstract
- **Title / author / year:** *Higher reversed sexual size dimorphism among nesting pairs of Northern Pygmy-Owls (Glaucidium gnoma) in northwestern Oregon than among specimens collected at the range-wide scale* — John F. Deshler, 2020.
- **DOI / URL / type / location / accessed:** https://doi.org/10.1676/19-132 ; https://www.researchgate.net/publication/351181106_Higher_reversed_sexual_size_dimorphism_among_nesting_pairs_of_Northern_Pygmy-Owls_Glaucidium_gnoma_in_northwestern_Oregon_than_among_specimens_collected_at_the_range-wide_scale ; journal abstract, lines 18–21; accessed 2026-11-07.
- **Facts:** Oregon breeding females averaged heavier and longer-winged than males; range-wide material overlapped more.
- **Limits / decision:** pre/current-split broad name and Oregon population make direct application to current Mountain *G. gnoma* uncertain. Supports only subtle female-fuller direction and rejection of a fixed standing-height ratio.

### NPO-MACAULAY-418673981-2008 — newly read institutional field record
- **Institution / contributor / date:** Cornell Lab Macaulay Library ML418673981; John Deshler; 11 June 2008.
- **URL / type / lines / accessed:** https://macaulaylibrary.org/asset/418673981 ; institutional field video record with observer notes, lines 54–106; accessed 2026-11-07.
- **Facts:** observer describes a 23–24-day nestling making a short flight from a stump nest with an unusually large natural side opening.
- **Limits / decision:** Oregon broad-name population, unusual nest opening, and unresolved current-species mapping. Not used for a day label, flight action, or large side-opening cavity in Mountain *G. gnoma* v3.

### NPO-DESHLER-MURPHY-2012-CONDOR-P314-322 — reused and freshly re-opened
- **Title / authors / year:** *The Breeding Biology of the Northern Pygmy Owl: Do the Smallest of the Small Have an Advantage?* — John F. Deshler & Michael T. Murphy, 2012.
- **DOI / URL / pages / type:** https://doi.org/10.1525/cond.2012.100140 ; https://academic.oup.com/condor/article/114/2/314/5152768 ; primary breeding study, pp.314–322.
- **Facts:** c.30-day incubation and mean c.27-day nestling period in northwestern Oregon.
- **Limits / decision:** broad-name Oregon study and no daily plumage series; not used to reconstruct current Mountain *G. gnoma* feather chronology.

### Reuse, conflict, and design boundary
- Reused repository page-verified Mikkola printed p.375/PDF p.377, ORI and Owl Pages evidence. The uploaded guide itself was unavailable in this execution.
- Direct v2 inspection found already-corrected neutral-dark juvenile iris but an oversized theatrical central hollow and adult–juvenile–adult order. V3 uses adult male–adult female–supported mesoptile order, a modest illustrative woodpecker-cavity lip, and one adult three-quarter turn to expose a nape false-eye patch.
- Unknown: current-species juvenile age/iris/flight threshold and daily feather sequence; exact sex-specific standing height; measured cavity geometry. Scientific verification remains false.
- Source accounting: new verified 2; reused and freshly re-opened 1; earlier page-verified sources reused 3; source-conflict boundary added 1.


## 2026-11-07 — #164 *Strix hadorami* v3 — sex-size and nest-shelf juvenile correction

### DESERTOWL-BENDOV-2017 — newly read primary breeding study
- Ben Dov, Atar, Baruchi, Levi & Sapir, 2017, “Breeding biology of Desert Owl in Israel,” *Dutch Birding* 39:285–307.
- URLs: https://cris.haifa.ac.il/en/publications/breeding-biology-of-desert-owl-in-israel/ ; https://www.researchgate.net/publication/320517643_Breeding_Biology_of_Desert_Owl_in_Israel
- Accessed 2026-11-07; pp. 298–305, Table 4 p.299, plates 419–420 pp.300–301.
- Facts: wing flapping c.20–25 d; first stand on nest shelf c.25 d; first flight c.30 d; leave nest site c.34 d. Use: supported open-eyed c.25–30 d pale mesoptile on broad ledge, no airborne action. Limits: small Israeli sample; no exact feather sequence or universal cavity geometry.
- Link: [#164 v3 research](species/164-strix-hadorami-v3.md).

### DESERTOWL-KIRWAN-2015 — newly read primary taxonomy/morphometry study
- Kirwan, Schweizer & Copete, 2015, *Zootaxa* 3904(1):28–50. DOI: https://doi.org/10.11646/zootaxa.3904.1.2 ; open copy: https://nnhc.huji.ac.il/sites/default/files/nnhc/files/15-zootaxa-kirwan_schweizer_copete_compressed.pdf
- Accessed 2026-11-07; Table 1, pp.41–42, Figs.7–8.
- Facts: current *S. hadorami* is the widespread western population formerly called *S. butleri*; no pronounced sexual dimorphism, females only slightly larger on average; small sample and individual colour variation. Use: near-equal adult height, only subtle female fullness; reject v2 male-larger reversal.
- Link: [#164 v3 research](species/164-strix-hadorami-v3.md).

## 2026-11-07 — #170 *Glaucidium sanchezi* v3 — original diagnosis, current name and evidence-bounded immature

### TPO-LOWERY-NEWMAN-1949-P2-3 — newly read original species description
- **Title / authors / year:** “New Birds from the State of San Luis Potosí and the Tuxtla Mountains of Veracruz, México,” G. H. Lowery Jr. & R. J. Newman, 1949, *Occasional Papers of the Museum of Zoology* 22.
- **URL:** https://www.biologicaldiversity.org/campaigns/global_owl_project/descriptions/Glaucidium/Glaucidium%20sanchezi%20(Lowery%20%26%20Newman)%201949,%20Occasional%20Papers%20of%20the%20Museum%20of%20Zoology,%20Louisiana.PDF
- **Accessed / type:** 2026-11-07; primary taxonomic/specimen description; all four PDF pages opened, printed pp.2–3 used.
- **Species/stage:** adult male holotype, adult female comparison and age-uncertain immature/young material from north-eastern Mexico.
- **Facts:** tiny earless pygmy-owl; adult male olive/grey-brown with reduced pale forecrown flecks, false-eye nape marks, pale-spotted coverts and barred tail; adult female is distinctly more cinnamon/rufous, especially crown/nape/upperparts and underpart streaks. The immature diagnosis supports a plainer dusky-grey crown/nape and less adult-like head pattern, but the prepared skins do not establish exact age, living soft parts, posture, nest or flight.
- **Design/check use:** encode the documented male greyer versus female more rufous palette; keep structural standing height near-equal and female only subtly fuller. Use an explicitly age-unknown older immature with formed but compact/soft folded wing, shortened supported tail and full bilateral perch contact.
- **Limitations/confidence:** high for described skins and name; no growth sequence, quantified population sex ratio or locomotor test.

### TPO-UNAM-MZFC-1999 — newly read institutional specimen record
- **Institution/title/year:** Museo de Zoología Alfonso L. Herrera / UNAM, *Glaucidium sanchezi* specimen record; assigned 1999.
- **URL:** https://repositorio.unam.mx/contenidos/ficha/glaucidium-sanchezi-lowery-newman-1949-1689410
- **Accessed/type:** 2026-11-07; university museum collection metadata, page sections “Procedencia,” “Cita” and “Descripción.”
- **Facts/use:** independently confirms current specimen identity in the MZFC bird collection and the Lowery & Newman name; taxonomy/provenance only.
- **Limitations:** the opened record does not provide sex, age, measurements, plumage or development; no visual detail transferred.

### TPO-ITIS-555452-2026 — newly read current-name authority
- **Institution/accessed:** Integrated Taxonomic Information System, TSN 555452; accessed 2026-11-07.
- **URL:** https://itis.gov/servlet/SingleRpt/SingleRpt?print_version=PRT&search_topic=TSN&search_value=555452&source=to_print
- **Facts/use:** valid species *Glaucidium sanchezi* Lowery & R. J. Newman, 1949; verified-standards record referencing IOC v15.1 and Clements. Current/old-name mapping only.
- **Limitations:** no morphology, sex or juvenile evidence.

### Reuse and unresolved limits
- Reused repository record **TPO-MIKKOLA-2013-P384** and v2 research boundaries; the user-uploaded guide itself was unavailable in this execution, so no new page-reading claim is made.

- **Title / author / year:** “Descriptions of Five New Birds from Mexico,” Edward W. Nelson, 1901, *The Auk* 18(1).

- **Conflict / decision:** original type has five tail-spot series, while later species-level sources summarize six/seven bars. V3 uses six restrained bars as a midpoint design inference and records the variation; no universal count claim.
- **Limits/confidence:** high for the described type; historic skin colour names, uncertain printed sex and no living soft parts/development/nest.

## 2026-11-07 — #173 *Otus madagascariensis* v3 — full nest paper and bounded older immature
- **TSO-RENE-THORSTROM-2009-P87-88** — corrected from abstract-only to full-paper read. René de Roland & Thorstrom 2009, pp.87–88; https://www.biodiversitylibrary.org/page/56261159 . Exceptional Berenty ground nest: ~20 mm depression, four white eggs, no material, abandoned before any observed hatching. Ordinary Tsimembo tree holes averaged 5.8 ± 2.2 m (n=7), clutch 2.4 ± 0.9 (n=5); mean mass 108 g (n=8). Supports dry western habitat and a strict “do not generalize ground nest / no chick inference” rule.

## 2026-11-07 — #174 *Strix sartorii* v3 — original female type, flight-capable fledgling and current habitat
- **CIO-BAIRD-RIDGWAY-1874-P29** — Baird, Brewer & Ridgway 1874, printed p.29, full public-domain account read via Project Gutenberg: https://www.gutenberg.org/files/54169/54169-h/54169-h.htm . Adult female type USNM 43,131: blackish-sepia/clear white, darker and less reddish than Barred Owl, nearly black lower streaks, plain dirty-white face without dark bars/semicircles; wing 14.80, tail 9.00, culmen 1.05, tarsus 2.20, middle toe 1.60 in. - **CIO-PIEPLOW-ETAL-2020-P221-227** — newly indexed/re-read full paper, *Western Birds* 51:221–227, DOI 10.21199/WB51.3.3. Historic 600–3,000 m range; current Nayarit site relatively humid pine–oak near 1,650 m; repeated surveys found it only there. Habitat/status only.
- **CIO-EARBIRDING-2015-FLEDGLING** — newly indexed/re-read direct field account: https://earbirding.com/blog/archives/4905 . Begging fledgling directly reported flying out of sight and associated with two apparent feeding events. Supports flight capability for that photographed stage, not exact age or first-flight timing.
- **CIO-AOS-2021-P263** — newly indexed/revalidated checklist source, DOI 10.1093/ornithology/ukab037. Species rank, protonym and pine/pine–oak habitat; taxonomy only.
- **CIO-MEXICANBIRDING-2020-PHOTO** — newly read supplemental living-adult photograph/page, Antonio Robles, Rancho La Noria July 2020: https://www.mexicanbirding.com/cinereous-owl/ . Adult/habitat corroboration; sex unconfirmed and not a measurement study.
- Exact fledgling age/sex, feather chronology, nest/clutch, flight onset and species-specific sex-size ratio remain unknown. Mikkola upload unavailable. [Research](species/174-strix-sartorii-v3.md). Science=false.

- **Useful facts:** pp.31 states only a few reliable fledgling records and three adult colour morphs; sightings clustered mid-February–early April. pp.33 describes one juvenile inferred to have fledged near project start but gives no exact age or plumage chronology. pp.34–35 reports hundreds of potential holes in *Cynometra webberi* and *Brachylaena huillensis* and explicitly says the project failed to locate a nest.
- **Limits / confidence:** direct project narrative, not a measured development series; “fledged” timing is inferred. High for search outcome and habitat, moderate for timing. It supports an age-unknown branch-perched fledgling and forbids a claimed nest interior.

## 2026-11-07 — #188 *Otus semitorques* v3 source additions

### JPSO-NOHARA-HONDA-2025-P75-78
- **Title:** A Reliable Breeding Record of Collared Scops Owl in Saitama Prefecture, Japan
- **Authors / institution:** Kiyoshi Nohara; Rina Honda; Saitama Museum of Natural History
- **Year / type:** 2025; primary field record / museum bulletin
- **URL / DOI:** https://doi.org/10.24715/smnh.19.0_75
- **Accessed:** 2026-11-07
- **Taxon / stage:** *Otus semitorques*; Honshu adult and recent fledgling
- **Actually read:** printed pp.75–78 / PDF pp.1–4; adult Fig.1 and young Fig.3.
- **Facts:** adult had ear tufts, orange iris, feathered toes and a brownish-white hind-neck patch (printed p.76). The young was fully down-covered with distinct tufts; iris could not be determined. On 25–26 June 2024 it stayed on Japanese cedar at roughly 9–10 m, moved little, and had incompletely grown flight and tail feathers; authors inferred very recent nest departure. Nest was not found (pp.76–78).
- **Limits / confidence:** high for the photographed individuals and stage; exact day-age, sex, iris and flight ability are unknown. No nest structure can be reconstructed.
- **Design support:** stationary, fully supported down-rich recent fledgling with incomplete short wing/tail; orange-eyed, feather-toed adult; cedar / patchy broadleaf habitat. No flight or nest.
- **Links:** research/species/188-otus-semitorques-v3.md; design/concepts/188-otus-semitorques-v3.png.

### JPSO-TOYAMA-ETAL-2015-P841-847

- **Authors / institution:** Masahiro Toyama; Nobuhiko Kotaka; Itsuro Koizumi; Hokkaido University / FFPRI
- **Year / type:** 2015; peer-reviewed primary breeding study
- **URL / DOI:** https://hdl.handle.net/2115/60540 ; https://doi.org/10.1139/cjz-2015-0060
- **Accessed:** 2026-11-07
- **Taxon / stage:** *Otus semitorques* breeding population on Okinawa; eggs, nestlings and nest departure
- **Actually read:** repository manuscript PDF pp.1–27; journal citation pp.841–847; methods and results including study-site / nest monitoring.
- **Facts:** study monitored Japanese and Ryukyu scops owls in subtropical forest, using natural cavities and nest boxes; Japanese Scops Owl was the earlier-breeding dietary generalist. The evidence is population and breeding-context evidence, not a plumage plate.
- **Limits / confidence:** high for the Okinawa study population. Do not transfer Okinawa plumage, exact timing or nest-box appearance to the nominate Honshu fledgling.
- **Design support:** forest/cavity ecology only; no nest reconstruction in this plate.
- **Links:** research/species/188-otus-semitorques-v3.md.

## 2026-11-07 — #25 *Glaucidium brasilianum cactorum* v4 — direct 28–40-day fledgling correction

### CFPO-AGFD-1996-P22-23
- **Title / authors / year:** *Cactus Ferruginous Pygmy-Owl Surveys and Nest Monitoring in the Tucson Basin Area, Arizona—1996* — Abbate, Ditty, Richardson & Olding, 1996.
- **URL / accessed / type:** https://downloads.regulations.gov/FWS-R2-ES-2021-0098-0002/attachment_1.pdf ; Arizona Game and Fish direct nest-monitoring report; read 2026-11-07.
- **Species / stage / pages:** *G. b. cactorum*; adults and 28–40-day fledglings; printed pp.22–23.
- **Facts / design use:** 28–31-day fledglings fully feathered without down; lighter smaller face, chocolate/light-brown upperparts, whiter less-streaked underside, no adult crown flecks, short rufous barred tail, grayish indistinct nape patches lacking adult white outline; first-week flights usually ≤6 m and first-day perching unstable. Female slightly larger/more rufous; male darker charcoal in the monitored pair.
- **Limits:** one Arizona brood/pair for detailed colour and behaviour; fledgling sex and exact hatch date unknown. Supports v4 stage, support posture and correction of v2 iris/nape simplification, not universal quantitative ratios.

### CFPO-USFS-GTR43-2000-P7
- **Title / institution / year:** *Ecology and Conservation of the Cactus Ferruginous Pygmy-Owl in Arizona* — USDA Forest Service RMRS-GTR-43, Cartron & Finch eds., 2000.
- **URL / pages / type / accessed:** https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/GOVPUB-A13-PURL-gpo111726/pdf/GOVPUB-A13-PURL-gpo111726.pdf ; institutional technical synthesis with field photographs; printed p.7 and Fig.1-3; read 2026-11-07.
- **Facts / design use:** adult cactorum paler/grayer, lemon-yellow eyes, crown buff flecks, crisp black-white nape patches, plain rusty back, vertically streaked white breast, long rufous 7–8-bar tail. Reported averages c.64 g male / 75 g female; female more cinnamon-rufous. Recent fledgling shorter-tailed, chocolate-brown except rufous tail, lighter nape patches, no crown buff, more white below.
- **Limits:** several measurements compile unpublished data; used as direction and cross-check, not a rigid universal size ratio.

### CFPO-VANROSSEM-1937-P27-28
- **Title / author / year:** “The Ferruginous Pigmy Owl of Northwestern Mexico and Arizona” — A. J. van Rossem, 1937.
- **URL / pages / type / accessed:** https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/9/98/Proceedings_of_the_Biological_Society_of_Washington_%28IA_proceedingsofbi501937biol%29.pdf ; original subspecies description, pp.27–28; read 2026-11-07.
- **Facts:** type adult male from giant-cactus grove near Guaymas/Empalme; cactorum shorter-winged, longer-tailed and paler/grayer than *G. b. ridgwayi*.
- **Limits:** adult type series only; no juvenile chronology.

### CFPO-USFWS-FR2023-P46915
- **Title / institution / year:** Threatened species final rule for Cactus Ferruginous Pygmy-Owl — U.S. Fish and Wildlife Service, 2023.
- **URL / page / type / accessed:** https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/FR-2023-07-20/pdf/2023-14486.pdf ; regulatory taxonomic/morphology synthesis; p.46915; read 2026-11-07.
- **Facts / design use:** current U.S. treatment *G. b. cactorum*; male 58–66 g, female 70–75 g; yellow eyes, lightly streaked crown, dark white-outlined nape patches, long barred rufous tail; tree/columnar-cactus cavity nesting.
- **Limits:** synthesis, not a development experiment; supports current name and broad adult direction.

- **Links:** `research/species/25-glaucidium-brasilianum-cactorum-v4.md`; planned `design/concepts/25-glaucidium-brasilianum-cactorum-v4.png`; run `production/runs/20261107T212500+0800-cycle-6.md`.
- **Correction:** v2/v3 retained. Direct observation supersedes the complete absence of juvenile nape patches: 28–40-day birds have lighter indistinct gray patches, full feathering and a short rufous tail. Juvenile iris chronology is not stated; yellow in v4 is an explicit design inference, not a verified age cue. New4 / reused0 / corrected1; science false.
