## 2026-11-07 — #27 *Glaucidium passerinum* v5 recent-fledgling correction

### EUPO-PACENOVSKY-SOTNAR-2010-PP71-73
- Title: Notes on the reproduction, breeding biology and ethology of the Eurasian pygmy owl (*Glaucidium passerinum*) in Slovakia
- Authors: Samuel Pačenovský; Karol Šotnár
- Year: 2010; DOI: 10.2478/v10262-012-0046-y
- URL: https://www.researchgate.net/publication/269515436_Notes_on_the_reproduction_breeding_biology_and_ethology_of_the_Eurasian_pygmy_owl_Glaucidium_passerinum_in_Slovakia
- Accessed: 2026-11-07; type: primary multi-year field synthesis; verified full text
- Species/stage: *G. passerinum*, nestlings and recently fledged juveniles
- Pages/sections: printed pp.71–73; Table 3
- Valuable facts: nestlings appear at cavity entrance at 21–25 days; reported fledging 27–34 days; direct Slovak observations say newly fledged birds could fly but landed less skillfully, remained in the family territory, and were followed for 27 days. One detailed family moved 30 m on fledging day and was still provisioned.
- Limits/conflicts: does not provide a feather-by-feather plate, universal iris chronology or a single mandatory fledging day. Used to select a supported recent fledgling and avoid a precise day label or flight pose.
- Design links: research/species/27-glaucidium-passerinum-v5.md; design/concepts/27-glaucidium-passerinum-v5.png
- Confidence: high for behaviour/timing; not a photographic plumage source.

### EUPO-EGE-2024-FLEDGING
- Title: Der Sperlingskauz (*Glaucidium passerinum*)
- Institution: Gesellschaft zur Erhaltung der Eulen e.V. (EGE)
- Published/updated: 2024-11-24
- URL: https://egeeulen.de/eulenarten/der-sperlingskauz-glaucidium-passerinum/
- Accessed: 2026-11-07; type: specialist owl-conservation institutional account; verified page
- Species/stage: *G. passerinum*, adult and recently fledged young
- Section: Fortpflanzung; lines 65–87 in retrieved page
- Valuable facts: nestling period normally 28–32 days; young leave somewhat flight-capable even though major flight feathers are not fully developed; family remains together about six weeks. Adult diagnostic direction and spruce/woodpecker-cavity context are also summarized.
- Limits/conflicts: secondary institutional account, not individual-age plumage measurements; does not establish iris colour or exact feather geometry. Supports incomplete-flight-feather boundary only.
- Confidence: medium-high.

### EUPO-VASSEN-WMC-2014-JUVENILE
- Title: Juvenile Eurasian Pygmy Owl (*Glaucidium passerinum*), Eastern Belgium (14444712289)

## 2026-11-07 — #28 *Glaucidium perlatum* v5 — recent-fledgling age correction

### PSOW-ROBERTS-BDI-2005-2026
- **Title / authors / year:** *Glaucidium perlatum* (Pearl-spotted owlet); Biodiversity Explorer synthesis citing Hockey, Dean & Ryan (eds), *Roberts Birds of Southern Africa VII* (2005); live page accessed 2026-11-07.
- **URL:** https://www.biodiversityexplorer.info/birds/strigidae/glaucidium_perlatum.htm
- **Type / section:** institutional-linked regional secondary synthesis; Distribution and habitat; Breeding; page has no stable printed pagination.
- **Taxon / stage:** *Glaucidium perlatum*; adult habitat/nest and recently fledged dependent young.
- **Facts:** open thorn savanna/bushveld/sparse woodland; old natural or barbet/woodpecker cavity; young remain in the cavity 27–31/32 days, then live in dense bush near the nest and remain parent-fed at least 14 days.
- **Limits / conflict:** secondary synthesis; interval is not a primary daily plumage series and does not justify assigning one exact visible day-age.
- **Design support:** recent fledgling low in dense bush near the nesting tree; parent-dependent; no exact day-age or airborne pose.
- **Links:** research/species/28-glaucidium-perlatum-v5.md; design/concepts/28-glaucidium-perlatum-v5.png.

### PSOW-SANBI-2025
- **Title / author / date:** “Pearl-spotted owlet” — Nozipho Khumalo, SANBI Zoological Research, 2025-10-09.
- **URL:** https://www.sanbi.org/animal-of-the-week/pearl-spotted-owlet/
- **Accessed / type:** 2026-11-07; national biodiversity institution species account.
- **Sections / taxon / stage:** Description, Habitat, Sex and Family life; adult *Glaucidium perlatum* and nesting context.
- **Facts:** 17–21 cm, about 75 g; brown with heavy white spotting; paired dark false eyes; mopane/open thorn savanna; old barbet/woodpecker cavities.
- **Limits:** no sex-separated measurements or juvenile plumage sequence.
- **Design support:** adult identity, false-eye anatomy, habitat and cavity context only.
- **Links:** research/species/28-glaucidium-perlatum-v5.md.

### PSOW-OWLPAGES-2020
- **Title / author / update:** “Pearl-spotted Owlet ~ *Glaucidium perlatum*” — Deane Lewis, updated 2020-10-29.
- **URL:** https://www.owlpages.com/owls/species.php?s=1860
- **Accessed / type:** 2026-11-07; specialist secondary synthesis; Description/Breeding/Habitat.
- **Facts:** 29-day incubation; young hatch with closed eyes, open them around 12 days, leave near 31 days able to move short distances, hide near the nest and remain parent-fed.
- **Limits / conflict:** not a primary growth series; the 31-day behavioural boundary cannot be mapped to an exact day-specific visual feather state.
- **Design support:** recent-fledgling dependence and no long-distance/independent behaviour.
- **Links:** research/species/28-glaucidium-perlatum-v5.md.

- **Accessed:** 2026-11-07.
- **Status:** UNVERIFIED LEAD. Search extraction described finer/more obvious juvenile flight-feather barring and larger female feathers with otherwise same pattern, but direct retrieval returned 502.
- **Limits / design use:** not used for juvenile marking geometry or quantitative sex difference until the specimen page can be read directly.

## 2026-11-07 — #29 *Glaucidium capense* v5 — short-hop fledgling evidence

### ABOW-VENTER-BIRDLIFE-2019-PP23-28
- **Title / author / year:** “Enchanted evenings with African Barred Owlets” — Albie Venter, 2019.
- **Publication / pages:** *African Birdlife*, May/June 2019, pp.23–28; BirdLife South Africa.
- **URL:** https://www.birdlife.org.za/22-28_barred-owlets_new3/
- **Accessed / type:** 2026-11-07; direct field observation and photographic account.
- **Taxon/stage:** *Glaucidium capense*; adults, nestlings and newly fledged dependent young at Thornybush, Limpopo.
- **Facts:** natural rot hole in marula; post-departure chicks roosted in nearby trees, made only short hops, did not travel far, and stretched flight muscles; adults continued provisioning.
- **Limits:** exact age and sex of photographed chicks, daily feather/iris sequence and sustained-flight threshold are not supplied.
- **Design use:** low, fully supported recent fledgling making one short-wing stretch; no airborne pose or exact day claim.
- **Links:** research/species/29-glaucidium-capense-v5.md; design/concepts/29-glaucidium-capense-v5.png.


## 2026-11-08 — #32 *Glaucidium castanotum* v5 — current name, field identity and evidence-bounded older dependent

### CBOW-ITIS-2025
- **Title / institution / accessed:** “ITIS Report: *Glaucidium castanotum*” / Integrated Taxonomic Information System / accessed 2026-11-08.
- **URL:** https://www.itis.gov/servlet/SingleRpt/SingleRpt?search_topic=TSN&search_value=1063259
- **Type / scope:** government-integrated taxonomic authority; current name and synonymy.
- **Facts read:** *Glaucidium castanotum* (Blyth, 1851), TSN 1063259, is valid; *Glaucidium castanonotum* and *Athene castanotus* are synonyms; Chestnut-backed Owlet is the English common name.
- **Section / lines:** Taxonomy and Nomenclature, retrieved lines 42–71 and 87–110.
- **Limits / confidence:** nomenclature only; does not support plumage, development or sex-size direction. High confidence for name mapping.
- **Design use:** retain roster name *G. castanotum* and historical search mappings; no morphology inferred from taxonomy.

### V5 evidence reconciliation
- CBOW-EDIRISINGHE-2025 re-read at printed p.72 (PDF lines 1414–1425): seven wet-zone Chestnut-backed Owlets and eight dry-zone Jungle Owlets were captured; Chestnut-backed Owlet had a much redder mantle and fewer chest bars. The abstract does not age- or sex-stratify its sample.
- CBOW-CEYLONBIRDCLUB-2009 re-read at page lines 47–63: yellow-eyed, untufted wet-zone bird; 2001 and 2006 cavities held young; both adults fed young and chicks replied/peeped from the cavity. This supports pair care, cavity context and a dependent-young action, not a day-age or plumage sequence.
- CBOW-COMMONS-ABEY2019 and CBOW-FLICKR-KALUBOWILA-2014 reused for an age-unknown fully feathered naturally supported older juvenile. “Few months” remains an observer estimate.
- **Corrected boundary:** no source supports a quantified male/female standing-height difference. V5 keeps adults near equal; the juvenile is fully feathered, lower and supported, with no flight or independence claim.
- **Uploaded guide:** unavailable in this execution. The repository’s prior page-verified CBOW-MIKKOLA-2013 record (printed p.406) is reused without a new page claim.

- **DOI / URL / accessed:** https://doi.org/10.2307/4089365 ; https://www.biologicaldiversity.org/campaigns/global_owl_project/descriptions/Glaucidium/Glaucidium%20nubicola%20%28Robbins%20%26%20Stiles%29%201999%2C%20Auk%2C%20116%2C%20pages%20305-315%20%28front%20cover%29..pdf / 2026-11-08.

## 2026-11-11 — #108 *Otus rutilus* v5 — reopened guide and primary specimen boundary

### RUT-MIKKOLA-2013-P143-144 — corrected/expanded existing guide record
- Mikkola, *Owls of the World: A Photographic Guide*, 2nd ed. (2013), printed pp.143–144 / uploaded PDF pp.145–146; page text and images reopened 2026-11-11.
- Adult facts: 19–22 cm, 85–120 g, very small tufts, yellow eyes, feathered tarsi, three morphs, humid rainforest/brush and shorter tail than Torotoroka. Female average +18 g is a mass direction, not a fixed height ratio.
- Critical limit: the account says juvenile unknown. It cannot supply age, feather chronology or locomotor ability. Design use is restricted to adults and habitat.

### RUT-BENSON-COLEBROOK-WILLIAMS-1976 — new verified primary specimen record
- Benson, Colebrook-Robjent & Williams (1976), “A Contribution to the Ornithology of Madagascar,” *Oiseau et R.F.O.* 46:103–134.
- URL: https://protectedareas.mg/content/documents/95a20108-b580-4f0b-8c45-00bcec5dd791/59df4e079a9d49ac9ddcfc6a5d21633a.pdf
- Read: original PDF systematic account, parsed lines 825–846; exact subsection journal page not exposed by retrieval.
- Facts: two males had yellow irides; Sakaraha male 107 g. A 12-specimen western series tended paler/greyer and nine humid-east birds darker/tawnier, but overlap/exceptions were explicit; strongest rufous tendency occurred in central/northern humid east.
- Limit: predates the current east/west split and supplies no female or juvenile development evidence. Supports a cautious eastern rufous direction only.

Reused without duplicate IDs: `RUT-RASMUSSEN-ETAL-2000`, `RUT-ITIS-202268-2026`, `RUT-VASSEN-2013-ANDASIBE-JUV`, `RUT-CORNELL-ADULT-PHOTOS-2026`. Full prompt/boundaries: `owl-pet/research/species/108-otus-rutilus-v5.md`. Scientific verification remains false.


## 2026-11-11 — #115 *Otus lempiji* v4 — mobile juvenile behavior and lowland habitat

### SUN-LOK-2009 — corrected/expanded existing source record
- Lok, A. F. S. L., Lee, T. K. & Lim, K. C. (2009). “The Biology of *Otus lempiji cnephaeus*, the Sunda Scops-Owl in Singapore.” *Nature in Singapore* 2:31–38. LKCNHM/NUS.
- URL: https://lkcnhm.nus.edu.sg/app/uploads/2017/04/2009nis31-38.pdf ; accessed 2026-11-11.
- Printed p.32 Fig.2: juvenile lacking distinct ear tufts, supported on an Albizia twig. Printed pp.35–37 Figs.5–9 and text: two Oct.2008 immature birds begged on adult arrival; one flew to the adult branch and feeding/prey handling followed.
- Design support: an older age-unknown mobile juvenile may be branch-supported and shown begging. It does **not** establish exact age, sex, a universal fledging threshold or nominate *O. l. lempiji* juvenile colour.
- Confidence: high for the documented Singapore behavior; subspecies-transfer boundary explicit.

### SUN-NAJMI-HANIS-2016
- Najmi-Hanis, Z., Puan, C. L., Zakaria, M. & Azhar, B. (2016). “Home range and activity patterns of Sunda scops owl in Peninsular Malaysia.” *Raffles Bulletin of Zoology* 64:28–32.
- URL: https://lkcnhm.nus.edu.sg/app/uploads/2017/04/64rbz028-032.pdf ; accessed 2026-11-11.
- Primary radio-telemetry study of six molecularly sexed adults in isolated lowland Ayer Hitam forest. Species also described from forest, plantations and suburban/wooded habitats.
- Design support: lowland wooded-edge habitat only. No juvenile morphology, adult plumage or sex-size conclusion.
- Confidence: high for study habitat/activity; not a development source.

### SUN-PILLA-2018
- Pilla, P., Puan, C. L., Lim, V.-C., Azhar, B. & Zakaria, M. (2018). “Sunda Scops-Owl Density Estimation via Distance Sampling and Call Playback.” *Sains Malaysiana* 47(3):441–446. DOI: https://doi.org/10.17576/jsm-2018-4703-03
- URL: https://www.ukm.my/jsm/pdf_files/SM-PDF-47-3-2018/03%20Phillcaine%20Pilla.pdf ; accessed 2026-11-11.
- Primary lowland dipterocarp-forest survey: 58 detections, 72.41% during the breeding period.

- Camacho-Varela, Pablo. “¿Cómo identificar correctamente los búhos *Megascops* presentes en Costa Rica?” *Zeledonia* 18(2), 2014, pp.3–14. Asociación Ornitológica de Costa Rica.

- Limits: one historical adult female specimen description, archaic taxonomy and no juvenile development evidence.

- Current accepted species name: *Megascops cooperi* (Ridgway, 1878). Historical synonyms retained: *Otus cooperi* and *Scops cooperi*. Two direct subspecies are listed, *M. c. cooperi* and *M. c. lambi*.

### BCSO-MENQ-ANJOS-2015 — primary habitat study
- W. Menq & L. Anjos, 2015, “Habitat selection by owls in a seasonal semi-deciduous forest in southern Brazil,” *Brazilian Journal of Biology* 75(4 Suppl.1):S143–S149. DOI/URL: https://doi.org/10.1590/1519-6984.07614
- Accessed 2026-11-12. Primary field/playback habitat study. Full HTML article and PDF pp. S143–S149 read; abstract/methods and published p.146 were used. The study recorded 56 individuals of six owl species at Perobas Biological Reserve and measured canopy height, hollow trees, fallen trees and glades. *M. atricapilla* was not restricted to a single forest structure but was absent from the most open tree-poor point. High confidence for Atlantic-Forest structural context; it does not establish an active nest, sex ratio or juvenile stage. Supports #133 v5 background only.

## 2026-11-12 — #137 *Otus alfredi* v5 — juvenile specimen and adult sex-scale re-read

- **FLO-WIDODO-1999:** primary taxonomic/morphology paper re-read at printed pp.15–22, Tables1–2 and Fig.2. ## 2026-11-12 — #139 *Otus jolandae* v5 — retained-juvenile-feather correction

### JOL-SANGSTER-ETAL-2013
- **Title/authors/year/type:** “A New Owl Species of the Genus *Otus* (Aves: Strigidae) from Lombok, Indonesia” / Sangster, King, Verbelen & Trainor / 2013 / peer-reviewed primary original description.
- **DOI/URL/accessed:** https://doi.org/10.1371/journal.pone.0053712 ; https://journals.plos.org/plosone/article?id=10.1371/journal.pone.0053712 ; 2026-11-12.
- **Pages/sections:** article pp.1–13; online lines 155–173, 280–354, 374–388; Fig.1 and Fig.S1.
- **Facts:** adults are dark cinnamon brown above, with warm cinnamon breast contrasting with whitish finely barred belly; golden-yellow iris, dark/brown bill, dull-ochre feet, tarsal feathering to toe bases. One female had paler breast, narrower head streaking and larger pale median-covert spots. Female AMNH 629942 retained scattered white/buffy juvenile crown/nape feathers but was not described as an all-over downy chick. Habitat includes solid, degraded and secondary forest/tree patches from 25–1350 m.
- **Limits/design:** type series is only seven specimens; individual female differences are not a universal sex-size rule. Exact immature age, iris, feather chronology, flight and nest are unknown. V5 uses a near-adult supported immature with only scattered retained juvenile head feathers.

## 2026-11-12 — #141 *Otus magicus* v5 — museum-series juvenile-wing and tarsal-boundary correction

### MAG-FINSCH-1898-P163-172 — newly indexed primary museum series
- **Title / author / year:** “Über *Scops magicus* (S. Müll.) und die verwandten Arten” / Otto Finsch / 1898, *Notes from the Leyden Museum* 20:163–180, plates 9–10.
- **URL / accessed / type:** https://repository.naturalis.nl/pub/508788/NLM1898020002009.pdf ; accessed 2026-11-12; primary comparative museum study.
- **Pages actually read:** printed pp.163–172, especially pp.163–164 and 170–172.
- **Species/stages:** 94-specimen *Scops magicus* complex; Batjan/Halmahera `leucospilus`, younger male and downy/nest-plumage young.
- **Facts:** colour/pattern vary widely and age/sex dependence was not established. Downy young with remiges/rectrices at differing development show fine dark crossbars; remiges may be as distinctly barred as adults. Tarsi are usually feathered near the toe bases but some specimens, including nest-plumage birds, have only the upper approximately two-thirds feathered and the rear surface bare. Tail barring varies too much for a universal exact count.
- **Limits/conflicts/credibility:** historical prepared series and broad old complex, not a known-age live growth sequence. High for the described specimens; no exact age, iris, flight, dependence, modern population ratio or nest inference.
- **Design/review decision:** allow a short partly down-veiled developing barred wing without turning the young into a miniature adult; show near-toe tarsal feathering with small irregular exposure, not a precise metric band; avoid exact repeated adult pattern counts.
- **Links:** `research/species/141-otus-magicus-v5.md`; planned v5 concept/review/run.

### Reused/re-read stable evidence
- `MAG-SHARPE-1875-P70-PLVI`: pp.70–74 and Plate VI reopened for historical adult-female `leucospilus` pattern and greyish-white cheek/ear-coverts; not treated as a dated development series.
- `MAG-ITIS-555379-2026`: current valid *Otus magicus*, original/synonym *Strix magica*, and valid *O. m. leucospilus* crosswalk reopened; taxonomy only.
- `MAG-MIKKOLA-2013-P189-190`: repository-pinned prior read reused; uploaded guide unavailable this execution, so no fresh page claim.
- **Index delta:** new verified 1; reused/re-read 3; corrected boundaries 3 (juvenile wing pattern, tarsal-feather variability, non-universal adult pattern counts). Detailed source facts, design boundary and complete prompt: `research/species/141-otus-magicus-v5.md`. Scientific verification remains false.
